===== .changeset/r36a-first-run.md =====

R36a: the shipped example runs on the real driver.

- A run's base is the tree of `--base-commit`, read from git's object store,
  never a copy of the working folder: nothing untracked or ignored reaches a
  model, and a `core.autocrlf=true` checkout integrates (#148, #154). The
  run's own files join it with the bytes `create` read; one committed with
  other bytes is refused at admission.
- Admission refuses a tool grant the seat's driver does not declare, and
  every refusal that reads the base, before the worst-case cost (#145, #150).
- A failed driver or sandbox attempt is recorded as an `attempt-failed`
  decision with its source, layer, and first 400 characters; a retry park
  names it; the stopped standing carries `lastFailure`; the event stream
  sends `attempt-failed`; `createApiServer` takes a `log` sink (#149).
- The drafter's request names `.drafts/<run id>/` (#151).
- The Claude Code CLI is pinned at 2.1.310, which calls every tier's model;
  its tool inventory gains `DesignSync`, `Monitor`, and `PushNotification`
  (#152).

 - [examples/policy.yaml](examples/policy.yaml): a commented policy for a
   supervised (L1) run. It caps every run at L1; a person approves the spec,

   runs, it installs the lockfile (`package-lock.json` or `pnpm-lock.yaml`,
   every package pinned by hash) in a sandbox of its own with no lifecycle
   scripts, and the checks run over that install with no network. A
   repository that declares dependencies under a policy granting no registry,

   whose lockfile links a package to a directory of the repository (an npm or
   pnpm workspace, a `file:` directory) or patches one: either would put code

+A run is built over the tree of `--base-commit` as git stores it, never over
+the working copy: nothing uncommitted, untracked, or ignored there (a `.env`,
+`node_modules/`, a build output) reaches a model, and uncommitted changes do

+`create` names: they join that tree with the bytes `create` read. You need

+checkout its line endings do) is refused. A secret that is committed is in
+the tree; keep it out of every model's tree with the policy's

===== examples/roles/spec-drafter.md =====
diff --git a/examples/roles/spec-drafter.md b/examples/roles/spec-drafter.md

@@ -13,36 +13,36 @@ except the four files below.
 
 ## What you write
 
-Write exactly these four files in the one directory under `.drafts/` in your
-working directory, which the runtime made for this run and left empty:
+Write exactly these four files in the directory under `.drafts/` that your
+request names, which the runtime made for this run and left empty:
 
 - `spec.md`: one shippable capability, in statements each of which a test can
   check. Say what the change does, and what it must not do. Cover the intent
   and nothing beyond it.
 - `security.json`: the spec's security part. Either
   `{ "none": "<one line saying why the change touches no trust boundary>" }`,
   or `{ "trustBoundaries": [...], "sensitiveData": [...],
   "authorizationRules": [{ "id": "...", "rule": "..." }], "secrets": [...] }`,
   each list possibly empty. Name every authorization rule the change adds or
   relies on: each one will need a test that is refused by it.
 - `run-requirements.json`: what the acceptance tests need to run, for a test
   writer who will never see this repository:
   `{ "testCommand": ["..."], "framework": "...", "testDirectory": "...",
   "publicInterface": "..." }`. `testDirectory` is where the framework finds
   acceptance tests, relative to the repository root. `publicInterface` says
   exactly what a test calls and how to reach it from a file in
   `testDirectory`: module paths, function or command names, inputs, outputs.
 - `questions.json`: `{ "assumptions": [{ "point": "...", "chose": "..." }],
   "openQuestions": ["..."] }`. List each point the intent left undecided and
   what you chose. List as an open question anything you could not decide
   without guessing what the person wants.
 
 ## What you must not do
 
 - Do not settle an open question yourself. A spec with an open question goes
   to a person; that is the right outcome, not a failure.
 - Do not write tests, change the code, or write anywhere else in the tree.
   Nothing else you write is read.
 - Do not reach for anything the session does not offer you.
 
 ## How you are judged

  * drafter over a tree holding the locked spec and nothing else, so it is
  * black-box by what it can reach, not by its instructions (I3).

 async function draft(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, previous: DraftAttempt | null): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const drivers = draftingDrivers(ctx);
   const driver = seatDriver(drivers, station, 'drafter');
   const seat = seatTask(ctx.state, station, 'drafter', ctx.run.baseCommit);
   const capability = capabilityRefusal(STATION_CONTRACTS[station], driver.capabilities());
   if (capability !== undefined) return capability;
   if ((await ctx.components.vault.readLocks(ctx.run.id)) !== null) {
     const tampered = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
     if (tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, tampered, { phase: 'before-draft' }, null);
   }
   // At L3 a test drafter of the builder's family is refused, as a same-family reviewer is (R9, R19).

   }

     context = joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS.spec, withInstructions({ intent: ctx.drafting.intent ?? undefined } as Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>>, instructions)));
   } else {
     const spec = await readText(ctx, specArtifacts(ctx));
     if (spec.tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, spec.tampered, { phase: 'context' }, null);

   }

   const started = await startAttempt(ctx, seat);
   if (started !== undefined) return started;

 async function critique(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, record: DraftRecord): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {

   await omitWithheld(ctx, DRAFT_ROLES[station].critic, work);
   await writeFiles(work, record.files.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));

 export interface ApiServerOptions {

+/** One line a failed attempt is logged as. Control characters in the failure's text are spaced out, so it stays one line. */
+export function attemptLine(runId: RunId, task: TaskId, failure: AttemptFailure): string {

+  return `run ${runId} task ${task}: attempt failed (${failure.source} ${failure.layer}, retry ${String(failure.retries)}): ${detail}`;

 export interface ApiServer {
@@ -320,425 +332,429 @@ function refusalStatus(outcome: Exclude<RunOutcome, { ok: true }>): number {
 export function createApiServer(options: ApiServerOptions): ApiServer {
   if (typeof options.token !== 'string' || options.token.length < MIN_TOKEN_LENGTH) {
     throw new Error(`api server: the local token must be at least ${String(MIN_TOKEN_LENGTH)} characters`);
   }
   if (typeof options.principal !== 'string' || options.principal.trim() === '') throw new Error('api server: principal must name who the token is');
   const expected = digest(`Bearer ${options.token}`);
   const { components, principal } = options;
   const runs = new Map<RunId, Tracked>();
   const open = new Set<ServerResponse>();
   // The nightly jobs are given the sandbox, the scanners, and the Vault, and no builder or integrator (I2). The two
   // reviewer jobs seat the reviewer driver, under the roles runs are admitted with (R13b, D-R13b-03, D-R13b-07).
   const jobs = new NightlyJobs({
     vault: components.vault, sandbox: components.sandbox, workspaces: components.workspaces,
     limits: components.profile === null ? null : components.profile.limits, scanners: components.scanners,
     target: options.nightlyTarget ?? null,
     seat: {
       driver: components.reviewer, roles: options.rolesDirectory,
       image: components.profile === null ? 'none' : imageFor(components.profile, components.reviewer),
     },
   });
   const intake = new Intake({ vault: components.vault, secrets: options.secrets ?? (() => undefined), jobs });
   let scheduler: Scheduler | null = null;
 
   const tracked = (runId: RunId): Tracked => {
     let entry = runs.get(runId);
     if (entry === undefined) {
       entry = { drive: null, cancel: null, lastOutcome: null, streams: new Set() };
       runs.set(runId, entry);
     }
     return entry;
   };
 
   const publish = (entry: Tracked, event: RunEvent): void => {
     for (const stream of entry.streams) stream(event);
   };
 
   /** Drives a run in the background; the returned promise never rejects. */
   const drive = (runId: RunId, start: (hooks: DriveHooks) => Promise<RunOutcome>): void => {
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     entry.cancel = null;
     const hooks: DriveHooks = {
       cancelRequested: () => entry.cancel,
       onCommit: (state) => { publish(entry, { event: 'state', data: state }); },
+      onAttemptFailed: (task, failure) => {
+        publish(entry, { event: 'attempt-failed', data: { task, failure } });
+        options.log?.(attemptLine(runId, task, failure));
+      },
     };
     entry.drive = start(hooks)
       .then((outcome): DriveEnd => ({ kind: 'outcome', outcome }), (error: unknown): DriveEnd => ({ kind: 'error', message: describe(error) }))
       .then((end) => (end.kind === 'error' ? halt(runId, end) : end))
       .then(async (end) => {
         entry.lastOutcome = end;
         entry.drive = null;
         entry.cancel = null;
         const standing = await runStanding(components.vault, runId).then((s) => s.standing, () => undefined);
         if (standing !== undefined) publish(entry, { event: 'standing', data: standing });
         publish(entry, { event: 'end', data: end });
       });
   };
 
   /**
    * A drive that ended in an error, rather than a refusal the line committed,
    * left the run's state as it was mid-step, which status would read as open
    * and a cancel would relabel. The halt is committed so the stop is the
    * Vault's record, and survives this process (A-P9-02). A run already
    * cancelled keeps that record instead; if the halt cannot be committed, the
    * drive's end says so.
    */
   const halt = async (runId: RunId, end: DriveEnd & { kind: 'error' }): Promise<DriveEnd> => {
     try {
       const state = await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
       if (state.cancelled !== null || state.halted !== null) return end;
       await components.vault.commitRunState({ ...state, halted: { at: new Date().toISOString(), message: end.message } }, state.version);
       return end;
     } catch (error) {
       return { kind: 'error', message: `${end.message}; the halt could not be recorded: ${describe(error)}` };
     }
   };
 
   /** The per-run report, read from the Vault alone (D-I1b-07). */
   const report = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunReport> => {
     try {
       await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
     } catch (error) {
       throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no run ${runId}: ${describe(error)}`);
     }
     return runReport(components.vault, runId);
   };
 
   /**
    * The run's records as OTLP/JSON, for the client to send where its operator
    * chooses: the service itself never makes an outbound request (D-R12-02).
    */
   const telemetry = async (runId: RunId): Promise<TelemetryExport> => {
     try {
       await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
     } catch (error) {
       // A state that cannot be read is a fault, never absence (I5). A run refused at admission has no
       // state and is still exported; only a run with no record at all is absent.
       if (!isNoRunState(error)) throw error;
       if ((await components.vault.readDecisions(runId)).length === 0) throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no run ${runId}: ${describe(error)}`);
     }
     const result = await telemetryOf(components.vault, runId);
     if (!result.ok) throw new HttpError(409, 'not-finished', `run ${runId} has not finished, so its records may still change and it is not exported`);
     return result.export;
   };
 
   const view = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunView> => {
     // Taken before the read, not after: a drive that ends while the state is read has committed
     // past what was read, and a view saying `driving: false` beside that state would present a
     // stale standing as settled. Read first, a drive running at the start is still reported.
     const entry = runs.get(runId);
     const driving = entry?.drive != null;
     const lastOutcome = entry?.lastOutcome ?? null;
     let read: { state: RunState; standing: RunView['standing'] };
     try {
       read = await runStanding(components.vault, runId);
     } catch (error) {
       throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no run ${runId}: ${describe(error)}`);
     }
     return { runId, state: read.state, standing: read.standing, driving, lastOutcome };
   };
 
   const create = async (body: CreateRunBody): Promise<CreatedRun> => {
     const policy = await loadPolicyFile(options.policyFile);
     if (!policy.ok) throw new HttpError(422, 'refused', `the server's policy file is refused: ${policy.message}`, policy);
     const runId = randomUUID() as RunId;
     const { intent, ...request } = body;
     const admission = await admitRun({
       runId,
       ...request,
       // Only this route, authenticated as the principal, supplies an intent (R19, I7), a bug fix's as its parts (A-R16-02).
       intent: intent === undefined ? null : typeof intent === 'string' ? { text: intent, from: 'principal' } : { ...intent, from: 'principal', template: 'bug-fix' },
       rubrics: options.rubricsDirectory ?? null,
       policy: policy.policy,
       roles: options.rolesDirectory,
       components,
     });
     if (!admission.ok) throw new HttpError(refusalStatus(admission), 'refused', `run refused: ${admission.reason}`, admission);
     const { admitted } = admission;
     drive(runId, (hooks) => admitted.drive(hooks));
     return { runId, worstCase: admitted.worstCase, state: admitted.state };
   };
 
   const approve = async (runId: RunId, body: ApproveBody): Promise<RunView> => {
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     if (entry.drive !== null) throw new HttpError(409, 'conflict', `run ${runId} is being driven; it is not waiting for an approval`);
     await view(runId);
     const outcome = await approveStation({ runId, key: body.key, draft: body.draft ?? null, approvedBy: principal, vault: components.vault });
     if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'invalid-request' ? 400 : 409, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
     drive(runId, (hooks) => resumeRun({ runId, components }, hooks));
     return view(runId);
   };
 
   const reject = async (runId: RunId, body: RejectBody): Promise<RunView> => {
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     if (entry.drive !== null) throw new HttpError(409, 'conflict', `run ${runId} is being driven; it is not waiting for a decision on a draft`);
     await view(runId);
     const outcome = await rejectDraft({ runId, key: body.key, draft: body.draft, notes: body.notes ?? null, reason: body.reason ?? null, rejectedBy: principal, vault: components.vault });
     if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'invalid-request' ? 400 : 409, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
     // A draft sent back is drafted again; a parked run is driven only to record where it stopped.
     drive(runId, (hooks) => resumeRun({ runId, components }, hooks));
     return view(runId);
   };
 
   const cancel = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunView> => {
     await view(runId);
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     const inFlight = entry.drive;
     if (inFlight !== null) {
       entry.cancel = { by: principal, at: new Date().toISOString() };
       await inFlight;
       const after = await view(runId);
       if (after.state.cancelled === null) {
         throw new HttpError(409, 'refused', `run ${runId} finished before the cancellation was taken; there is nothing to cancel`, after.standing);
       }
       return after;
     }
     const outcome = await cancelRun({ runId, by: principal, vault: components.vault });
     if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'finished' ? 409 : 400, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
     const now = await view(runId);
     publish(entry, { event: 'state', data: now.state });
     publish(entry, { event: 'standing', data: now.standing });
     return now;
   };
 
   const events = async (runId: RunId, res: ServerResponse): Promise<void> => {
     const now = await view(runId);
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/event-stream; charset=utf-8', 'cache-control': 'no-store', connection: 'keep-alive' });
     open.add(res);
     const write = (event: RunEvent): void => {
       res.write(`event: ${event.event}\ndata: ${JSON.stringify(event.data)}\n\n`);
     };
     const finish = (): void => {
       entry.streams.delete(stream);
       open.delete(res);
       res.end();
     };
     const stream = (event: RunEvent): void => {
       write(event);
       if (event.event === 'end') finish();
     };
     write({ event: 'state', data: now.state });
     write({ event: 'standing', data: now.standing });
     if (entry.drive === null) {
       write({ event: 'end', data: entry.lastOutcome });
       open.delete(res);
       res.end();
       return;
     }
     entry.streams.add(stream);
     res.on('close', () => {
       entry.streams.delete(stream);
       open.delete(res);
     });
   };
 
   /**
    * `POST /events/<subscription>`, the one route outside the local token: a
    * delivery authenticates by its signature (D-A-TF-06). The subscription is
    * checked before the body is read, and nothing a refusal answers with is
    * the body's text.
    */
   const deliver = async (req: IncomingMessage, res: ServerResponse, id: string): Promise<void> => {
     const policy = await loadPolicyFile(options.policyFile);
     // A policy that does not load grants no subscription; nothing is read or recorded against it (I5).
     if (!policy.ok) throw new HttpError(503, 'refused', 'the server has no policy that loads, so no subscription is granted');
     const subscription = intake.subscription(policy.policy, id);
     let outcome: DeliveryOutcome;
     if (subscription === null) {
       outcome = await intake.count(UNSUBSCRIBED_BUCKET, 'not-subscribed');
       discard(req, MAX_DELIVERY_BYTES);
     } else {
       const body = await readCapped(req, MAX_DELIVERY_BYTES);
       if (body === 'oversize') {
         outcome = await intake.count(subscription.id, 'oversize');
         discard(req, MAX_DELIVERY_BYTES);
       } else {
         const signature = req.headers['x-hub-signature-256'];
         outcome = await intake.deliver(policy.policy, subscription, typeof signature === 'string' ? signature : undefined, body);
       }
     }
     send(res, deliveryStatus(outcome), outcome satisfies DeliveryAnswer);
   };
 
   const listIntake = async (): Promise<CandidateSummary[]> => {
     const entries = await components.vault.readTriggerDecisions();
     return entries.flatMap((entry) => (entry.candidate === null ? [] : [summary({ ...entry, candidate: entry.candidate })]));
   };
 
   const readIntake = async (id: string): Promise<CandidateView> => {
     const entry = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(id) ? await components.vault.readCandidate(id as CandidateId) : null;
     if (entry?.candidate == null) throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no intake candidate ${id}`);
     const c = entry.candidate;
     return { ...summary({ ...entry, candidate: c }), body: c.body, signedAt: c.signedAt, decisionSha256: entry.sha256 };
   };
 
   /** A person's dismissal: once, of a candidate neither admitted nor expired, with a cause and no text (A-R16-05). */
   const dismissIntake = async (id: string, body: DismissBody): Promise<CandidateSummary> => {
     const entry = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(id) ? await components.vault.readCandidate(id as CandidateId) : null;
     if (entry?.candidate == null) throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no intake candidate ${id}`);
     if (entry.expiredAt !== null) throw new HttpError(409, 'conflict', `candidate ${id} expired at ${entry.expiredAt}; an expired candidate is already recorded as not worked`);
     if (!(await components.vault.dismissCandidate(entry.candidate.id, body.cause, new Date().toISOString()))) {
       throw new HttpError(409, 'conflict', `candidate ${id} was admitted or dismissed before`);
     }
     const after = await components.vault.readCandidate(entry.candidate.id);
     if (after?.candidate == null) throw new Error(`server: candidate ${id} is gone after its dismissal`);
     return summary({ ...after, candidate: after.candidate });
   };
 
   /**
    * `GET /readiness`: the scan's report for one revision, as a stream. A bad
    * revision is refused before the stream opens; then `started` names the
    * commit, a comment every 15 s keeps a client waiting through a scan that
    * outlasts its header timeout, and one `report` or `failed` event ends it.
    * Read-only: nothing is written, and admission does not read the ceiling.
    */
   const readiness = async (revision: string, res: ServerResponse): Promise<void> => {
     const scanner = options.readiness ?? null;
     if (scanner === null) throw new HttpError(409, 'refused', 'this server scans no repository; readiness is served by factory-host over its FACTORY_REPOSITORY');
     let commit: string;
     try {
       commit = await scanner.resolve(revision);
     } catch (error) {
       throw new HttpError(400, 'bad-request', error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error));
     }
     res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/event-stream; charset=utf-8', 'cache-control': 'no-store', connection: 'keep-alive' });
     open.add(res);
     res.write(`event: started\ndata: ${JSON.stringify({ revision, commit })}\n\n`);
     const beat = setInterval(() => { res.write(': scanning\n\n'); }, 15_000);
     try {
       const report = await scanner.scan(commit);
       res.write(`event: report\ndata: ${JSON.stringify(report)}\n\n`);
     } catch (error) {
       res.write(`event: failed\ndata: ${JSON.stringify({ message: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) })}\n\n`);
     } finally {
       clearInterval(beat);
       open.delete(res);
       res.end();
     }
   };
 
   const route = async (req: IncomingMessage, res: ServerResponse): Promise<void> => {
     const url = new URL(req.url ?? '/', 'http://server.invalid');
     const parts = url.pathname.split('/').filter((p) => p !== '');
     const method = req.method ?? 'GET';
     if (parts[0] === 'events') {
       if (parts.length !== 2 || method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no route ${url.pathname}`);
       await deliver(req, res, decodeURIComponent(parts[1] ?? ''));
       return;
     }
     const presented = digest(req.headers.authorization ?? '');
     if (!timingSafeEqual(presented, expected)) throw new HttpError(401, 'unauthorized', 'a valid local token is required');
     if (parts[0] === 'intake' && parts.length === 3 && parts[2] === 'dismiss') {
       if (method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await dismissIntake(decodeURIComponent(parts[1] ?? ''), dismissBody(await readJson(req))));
       return;
     }
     if (parts[0] === 'intake' && parts.length <= 2) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, parts.length === 1 ? await listIntake() : await readIntake(decodeURIComponent(parts[1] ?? '')));
       return;
     }
     if (parts[0] === 'nightly' && parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await components.vault.readNightly());
       return;
     }
     if (parts[0] === 'outcomes' && parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await outcomes(components.vault, outcomeWindow(url.searchParams)));
       return;
     }
     if (parts[0] === 'readiness' && parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       await readiness(url.searchParams.get('revision') ?? 'HEAD', res);
       return;
     }
     if (parts[0] !== 'runs' || parts.length > 3) throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no route ${url.pathname}`);
     if (parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 201, await create(createBody(await readJson(req))));
       return;
     }
     const runId = decodeURIComponent(parts[1] ?? '') as RunId;
     const action = parts[2];
     if (action === undefined) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await view(runId));
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'report') {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await report(runId));
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'telemetry') {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await telemetry(runId));
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'events') {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       await events(runId, res);
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'approve' || action === 'cancel' || action === 'reject') {
       if (method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       if (action === 'approve') send(res, 200, await approve(runId, approveBody(await readJson(req))));
       else if (action === 'reject') send(res, 200, await reject(runId, rejectBody(await readJson(req))));
       else send(res, 200, await cancel(runId));
       return;
     }
     throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no route ${url.pathname}`);
   };
 
   const server: Server = createServer((req, res) => {
     route(req, res).catch((error: unknown) => {
       const http = error instanceof HttpError ? error : new HttpError(500, 'internal', describe(error));
       if (res.headersSent) {
         res.end();
         return;
       }
       sendError(res, http);
     });
   });
 
   return {
     listen: async (port, host) => {
       // The schedule is the service's: what a stop missed is recorded before the first request is taken (D-A-TF-08).
       scheduler ??= await startSchedules(async () => {
         const loaded = await loadPolicyFile(options.policyFile);
         return loaded.ok ? loaded.policy : null;
       }, components.vault, intake);
       return new Promise((resolveUrl, reject) => {
         server.once('error', reject);
         server.listen(port, host, () => {
           server.off('error', reject);
           const address = server.address();
           if (address === null || typeof address === 'string') {
             reject(new Error('api server: listening on something other than a TCP port'));
             return;
           }
           const shown = address.family === 'IPv6' ? `[${address.address}]` : address.address;
           resolveUrl(`http://${shown}:${String(address.port)}`);
         });
       });
     },
     close: async () => {
       scheduler?.stop();
       scheduler = null;
       for (const res of open) res.end();
       open.clear();
       await new Promise<void>((done) => {
         server.close(() => { done(); });
         server.closeAllConnections();
       });
       await Promise.all([...runs.values()].flatMap((entry) => (entry.drive === null ? [] : [entry.drive])));
     },
   };
 }

 /**
  * Starts an attempt at a task, counted and committed before the driver is
  * called. A task already `running` was in flight when the run stopped: the
  * attempt is run again, so it costs no iteration — but it is another
  * invocation, and `starts` counts it. Past the station's invocation bound the
  * task parks rather than being replayed into the same attempt forever
  * (A-P4-06).
  */
 export async function startAttempt(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const contract = STATION_CONTRACTS[ctx.state.station];
   const spent = attemptsOf(ctx.state, task);
   const replay = statusOf(ctx.state, task) === 'running';
   const attempt: TaskAttempts = replay
     ? { ...spent, starts: spent.starts + 1 }
     : { iterations: spent.iterations + 1, retries: 0, starts: spent.starts + 1, conflicts: spent.conflicts };
   if (attempt.starts > maxStarts(contract, spent.conflicts.length)) {
     return park(ctx, task, { attempts: { [task.id]: spent } }, 'starts-exhausted');
   }
   // An escalation is a decision, recorded before the attempt that carries it is committed, as a park
   // is: a stop between the two leaves a record of an escalation that did not take effect, and the
   // resume decides it again from the same counts (A-R14-02, D-P14-07). A replay is the same iteration.
   const escalation = replay ? null : escalationAt(scopeFor(ctx, task), ctx.state.station, attempt);
   if (escalation !== null) {
     await recordDecision(
       { vault: ctx.components.vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: task.id, station: ctx.state.station },
       { cause: 'tier-escalated', decidedBy: 'line', ...escalation },
     );
   }
   await commit(ctx, {
     tasks: { [task.id]: 'running' },
     attempts: { [task.id]: attempt },
   });
   return undefined;

+export const FAILURE_DETAIL_LIMIT = 400;
+
+/**
+ * What a failed attempt's error says, read by the runtime: a driver's or a
+ * sandbox's refusal by its name and layer, anything else as `error`. The
+ * text is the error's own and may quote the CLI's or the container's output,
+ * so it is trimmed and kept as untrusted data (I7, D-R36a-05).
+ */
+export function attemptFailure(error: unknown, retries: number): AttemptFailure {
+  const named = typeof error === 'object' && error !== null ? (error as { name?: unknown; layer?: unknown }) : {};
+  const layer = typeof named.layer === 'string' && named.layer !== '' ? named.layer : 'error';
+  const source = named.name === 'DriverRefusal' ? 'driver' : named.name === 'SandboxRefusal' ? 'sandbox' : 'runtime';
+  const text = (error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)).trim();
+  return { source, layer: source === 'runtime' ? 'error' : layer, retries, untrustedDetail: text.length > FAILURE_DETAIL_LIMIT ? `${text.slice(0, FAILURE_DETAIL_LIMIT)}…` : text };

 async function build(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {

   const offered: Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>> = {
     'locked-spec': spec.text,
     'acceptance-tests': tests.text,
     'task-graph': JSON.stringify(ctx.graph.tasks.map(({ id, station, role, dependsOn }) => ({ id, station, role, dependsOn }))),

   if (problems.length > 0) {
     throw new Error(`line: the driver's result for task ${task.id} does not match the TaskResult contract, so it was not recorded: ${problems.join('; ')}`);

 /**
  * `verify`: the task's own diff, held to the locks and the grant; then the
  * checks, run over a tree built from base and the cumulative diff, where the
  * agent never ran, mounted read-only with no egress. The verdict follows the
  * check results and nothing else (I2).
  */
 async function verify(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const { sandbox, vault, driver, workspaces } = ctx.components;
   const before = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
   if (before.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'verify', task, before, { phase: 'before-checks' });
   const result = await readResult(ctx, task);
 
   const { start, work } = attemptPaths(workspaces, ctx.run.id, task.id, attemptsOf(ctx.state, task).iterations);
   const written = await ownDiff(start, work);
   // A path the role is not given, written or created, is refused: an omitted file is never replaced unseen (D-R10-05).
   const sensitive = withheldOf(withheldGlobs(ctx, task.role), written.map((entry) => entry.path));

   const own = written.filter((entry) => !inDependencyTree(entry.path));
   const notCarried = [...new Set(written.filter((entry) => inDependencyTree(entry.path)).map((entry) => dependencyEntry(entry.path)))].sort();

   // A locked path in it is the tamper the Vault's own check can no longer see.

   if (outside.length > 0) return recordEscape(ctx, task, outside);
 
   const prior = await accepted(ctx);
   // I5: a path a sibling's accepted change touched after this task started would be overwritten by
   // composing; the task is rebuilt over the accepted tree instead, and no side is picked (R4).
   const conflict = await conflictOf(ctx, task, start, own, prior);

       : requiredShortfall(check, results[i]);

     // halt the claim could trigger would let the model's story decide the outcome (D-P6-03).

     contractVersion: driver.contractVersion,
   };

   // an iteration a conflict sent back was judged by no gate and spends none of them (D-R4-03).
   const spent = gatedIterations(attemptsOf(ctx.state, task));
   const status: TaskStatus = failed.length === 0 ? 'passed' : spent >= STATION_CONTRACTS[task.station].maxIterations ? 'parked' : 'failed';

   if (failed.length > 0) {

     );
   }
   // The station machine returns this park to the line on its next step; it is recorded here, first.
   if (status === 'parked') await park(ctx, task, { evidence: ref }, 'iterations-exhausted');
   else await commit(ctx, { tasks: { [task.id]: status }, evidence: ref });
   return undefined;
 }

   // the seat sees one. It is the runtime's diff, never the claim's file list (codex-5).
   const current = await accepted(ctx);

     'locked-spec': spec.text,

 /**
  * One decision's typed fields. Every cause is listed, so a cause added later
  * fails to compile here rather than reaching the seat unprojected. No
  * message, detail, host, path, check id, or task id is taken: a check id may
  * be one a drafter wrote (R19), a host is the agent's choice, and a task id is
  * the requester's text, admitted with no character class (I7).
  */
 function decisionFields(d: EnforcementDecision, violation: IntegrityViolation | null): Record<string, string | number | null> {
   const base = { cause: d.decision.cause, decidedBy: d.decision.decidedBy, station: d.station };
   const decision = d.decision;
   switch (decision.cause) {
     case 'record-drift': return { ...base, problem: decision.problem };
     case 'observation-closed': return { ...base, windowMs: decision.windowMs };
     case 'admission-refused': return { ...base, reason: decision.refusal.reason };
     case 'resume-refused': return { ...base, reason: decision.refusal.reason };
     case 'station-refused': {
       const refusal = decision.refusal;
       if (refusal.reason === 'gate-failed') {
         return { ...base, reason: refusal.reason, failedChecks: refusal.failed.length, failedCauses: [...new Set(refusal.failed.map((f) => f.cause))].sort().join(',') };
       }
       return { ...base, reason: refusal.reason };
     }
     case 'approval-granted': return { ...base, key: decision.key };
     case 'approval-refused': return { ...base, reason: decision.reason };
     case 'approval-rejected': return { ...base, reason: decision.reason, notes: decision.notes ? 'yes' : 'no' };
     case 'draft-sent-back': return { ...base, sentBackBy: decision.sentBack.by };
     case 'draft-floor-applied': return { ...base, level: decision.level, failedConditions: [...decision.failed].sort().join(',') };
     case 'egress-connection': return { ...base, verdict: decision.connection.verdict };
     case 'violation-recorded': return { ...base, violation: violation?.kind ?? null, role: violation?.role ?? null };
     case 'relay-refused': return { ...base, refused: decision.refused, exhausted: decision.exhausted };
     case 'tier-escalated': return { ...base, from: decision.from, to: decision.to, failedGates: decision.failedGates };
     case 'task-conflict': return { ...base, with: decision.with.length, paths: decision.paths.length };
+    // The failure's text quotes the CLI's or a container's output, so only the runtime's reading of it is taken (I7).
+    case 'attempt-failed': return { ...base, source: decision.failure.source, layer: decision.failure.layer, retries: decision.failure.retries };
     case 'capability-narrowed': return { ...base, narrowedBy: decision.narrowing.kind, level: decision.level, roles: Object.keys(decision.scopes).length };
   }
 }

 /**
  * The likely figure beside the worst case (R2): each task the worst case
  * counts, priced at the model its station's driver resolves the task's
  * starting tier to, from past runs' usage. Escalation is not modelled.
  */
 async function likelyCost(components: ComponentGraph, worstCase: WorstCaseCost, policy: Policy): Promise<CostEstimate> {
   const cells: EstimateCell[] = [];
   for (const task of worstCase.tasks) {
     if (!isAgentStation(task.station)) continue;
     const resolved = engine.resolveCapabilities(task.role, task.station, policy);
     // `worstCaseCost` resolved every one of these scopes, and throws where one does not.
     if (!resolved.ok) throw new Error(`likely cost: role ${task.role} has no scope at ${task.station}: ${resolved.detail}`);

     const driver = driverOfTask(task, components);
     if (driver !== undefined) cells.push({ station: task.station, model: driver.resolveModel(tier) });
   }
   return estimateCost(components.vault, cells);

+/**
+ * A tool a seated role is granted that the driver its station seats does not
+ * declare, checked for every seat the worst case counts (I4, D-R36a-07).
+ * Policy validation accepts any tool name, and a driver refuses a grant it
+ * does not declare only when the task is driven, after the spend; here the
+ * run is refused before the estimate, naming the tool and the driver.
+ */
+function toolGrantRefusal(worstCase: WorstCaseCost, policy: Policy, components: ComponentGraph): Extract<RunOutcome, { reason: 'policy-refused' }> | undefined {
+  for (const task of worstCase.tasks) {
+    if (!isAgentStation(task.station)) continue;
+    const driver = driverOfTask(task, components);
+    if (driver === undefined) continue;
+    const resolved = engine.resolveCapabilities(task.role, task.station, policy);
+    // `worstCaseCost` resolved every one of these scopes, and throws where one does not.
+    if (!resolved.ok) throw new Error(`tool grants: role ${task.role} has no scope at ${task.station}: ${resolved.detail}`);
+    const verdict = validateToolGrants({ ...policy, roles: { [task.role]: resolved.scope } }, driver.declaredTools());
+    if (verdict.ok) continue;
+    const tools = verdict.ungranted.map((u) => `'${u.tool}'`).join(', ');
+    const declared = driver.declaredTools();
+    return {
+      ok: false,
+      reason: 'policy-refused',
+      station: task.station,
+      role: task.role,
+      refusal: {
+        ok: false,
+        reason: 'capability-missing',
+        detail: `role ${task.role} is granted ${tools} at ${task.station}, which the driver ${driver.id} does not declare; ` +
+          (declared.length === 0 ? 'it declares no tool' : `it declares ${declared.map((t) => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}`),
+      },
+    };
+  }
+  return undefined;

 /**
  * Every admitted artifact whose path a sensitive class matches (D-R10-09). A
  * locked artifact is offered to seats as text in their context, which tree
  * omission cannot reach, so classifying one sensitive would withhold nothing.
  */
+/** Every artifact a request admitted, by path and hash, once each. */
+function admittedEntries(artifacts: unknown): AdmittedArtifact[] {
+  const found = new Map<string, string>();
+  const walk = (value: unknown): void => {
+    if (Array.isArray(value)) value.forEach(walk);
+    else if (typeof value === 'object' && value !== null) {
+      const record = value as Record<string, unknown>;
+      if (typeof record.path === 'string' && typeof record.sha256 === 'string') found.set(record.path, record.sha256);
+      else Object.values(record).forEach(walk);
+    }
+  };
+  walk(artifacts);
+  return [...found].sort(([a], [b]) => (a < b ? -1 : a > b ? 1 : 0)).map(([path, sha]) => ({ path, sha256: sha }));
+}

+/**
+ * Writes each admitted artifact into the base with the bytes admission hashed
+ * (D-R36a-10). The base is the commit's tree, and a run's artifacts are
+ * usually files the person wrote and did not commit; the line reads every one
+ * of them in every tree it builds. One the commit holds must be the bytes
+ * admitted, or what merges would be another tree than the one verified: a
+ * checkout whose line endings differ from its commit's is refused here,
+ * before any spend, and never at `integrate` after it (#154).
+ */
+async function overlayArtifacts(base: string, workspace: string, artifacts: unknown): Promise<RequestProblem[]> {
+  const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];

+  for (const artifact of admittedEntries(artifacts)) {

+    if (bytes === null || sha256(bytes) !== artifact.sha256) {
+      problems.push({ path: 'artifacts', code: 'changed', message: `${artifact.path} changed in the workspace while the run was being admitted` });

+    }

+      problems.push({
+        path: 'artifacts', code: 'differs-from-commit',
+        message: `${artifact.path} is committed at the base commit with other bytes than the workspace holds; commit it as it stands, or leave it out of the commit (a checkout with core.autocrlf=true writes other line endings than the commit holds)`,

+      continue;
+    }
+    files.push({ path: artifact.path, bytes });
+  }
+  if (problems.length === 0) await writeFiles(base, files);
+  return problems;

 /**
  * Where a run stands, derived from its committed state by the rules the line
  * itself follows (I2). Nothing a model wrote is an input.
  *
  * - `passed`: every M1 exit gate is crossed; a resume would return at once.
  * - `stopped`: the line refuses to continue, and says why.
  * - `awaiting-approval`: the next exit needs a human's approval of `key`;
  *   `escalations` says what raised it beyond its floor, if anything (A-I1a-07),
  *   and `draft` what is being decided at a drafted exit (R19).
  * - `open`: there is work left, and a drive would do it.

-  | { readonly standing: 'stopped'; readonly refusal: StationRefusal }

+/** A failed attempt as `status` and `events` show it. `untrustedDetail` is the CLI's or a container's text: shown, never prompted (I7). */
+export interface LastFailure {
+  readonly task: TaskId;
+  readonly station: StationId | null;
+  readonly decidedAt: string;
+  readonly failure: AttemptFailure;
+}
+
+/** The last recorded failed attempt of the task `refusal` parked for its retries, or null. */
+async function lastFailureOf(vault: Vault, runId: RunId, refusal: StationRefusal): Promise<LastFailure | null> {
+  if (refusal.reason !== 'parked' || refusal.cause !== 'retries-exhausted') return null;
+  let last: LastFailure | null = null;
+  for (const ref of await vault.readDecisions(runId)) {
+    const d = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(ref))) as EnforcementDecision;
+    if (d.decision.cause !== 'attempt-failed' || d.taskId !== refusal.task) continue;
+    if (last === null || d.decidedAt >= last.decidedAt) last = { task: d.taskId, station: d.station, decidedAt: d.decidedAt, failure: d.decision.failure };
+  }
+  return last;
+}
+
 export async function runStanding(vault: Vault, runId: RunId): Promise<{ state: RunState; standing: RunStanding; source: StandingSource }> {
+  const read = await standingOf(vault, runId);
+  if (read.standing.standing !== 'stopped') return { ...read, standing: read.standing };
+  return { ...read, standing: { ...read.standing, lastFailure: await lastFailureOf(vault, runId, read.standing.refusal) } };
+}
+
+type BareStanding = Exclude<RunStanding, { standing: 'stopped' }> | { readonly standing: 'stopped'; readonly refusal: StationRefusal };
+
+async function standingOf(vault: Vault, runId: RunId): Promise<{ state: RunState; standing: BareStanding; source: StandingSource }> {
   const state = await vault.readRunState(runId);
   const { record, policy } = await readAdmission(vault, state);
   // What was committed is read before what the workspace holds now. A cancellation, a halt, and the grant
   // spent in the commit that ended a passed run, are records an artifact edited afterwards does not
   // undo; re-hashed first, a later edit would report either run as tampered (P9 review, codex-3).
   if (state.cancelled !== null || state.halted !== null) {
     // `nextStep` refuses a cancelled or halted run before any other check, so no graph is needed to read why (A-P9-01, A-P9-02).
     const ended = nextStep(state, { tasks: [], edges: [] }, policy.concurrency);
     if (ended.kind === 'refuse') return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: ended.refusal }, source: 'records' };
   }
   // The last exit is crossed only by a run that passed, and when it needs an approval the line
   // spends the grant in the commit that ends the run. A spent grant for it is that record; the
   // gate re-evaluated would ask for a second approval of an exit already crossed (D-P9-08).
   const last = approvalKey(LAST_STATION, levelOf(record.run.requestedLevel, state.narrowed));
   if (state.approvals.some((grant) => grant.key === last && grant.usedAt !== null)) return { state, standing: { standing: 'passed' }, source: 'records' };
   const { graph, changed, drafting } = await loadLine(vault, record, state);
   if (changed.length > 0) {
     const paths = changed.map((c) => c.path).join(', ');
     return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: { ok: false, reason: 'lock-tamper', tampered: changed, message: `an admitted artifact changed: ${paths}` } }, source: 'workspace' };
   }
   // The policy's own limits: the standing reads only a refusal or an exit, which the batch size does not change (D-R4-02).
   const step = nextStep(state, graph, policy.concurrency);
   if (step.kind === 'refuse') return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: step.refusal }, source: 'workspace' };
   if (step.kind !== 'exit') return { state, standing: { standing: 'open' }, source: 'workspace' };
   const floor = floorAt(state, drafting);
   const held = locksHeldLeaving(step.from) && (await vault.readLocks(runId)) !== null;
   const verdict = held ? await vault.verifyLocks(runId) : { ok: true as const };
   // The same escalation the line applies, from the same bundles, so the standing and a drive agree.
   const escalations = step.from === 'integrate'
     ? await integrateEscalations(vault, state, async (ref) => JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(ref))) as EvidenceBundle)
     : { protectedPathsTouched: [], tamperFindings: [] };
   const next = transition({
     from: step.from,
     to: step.to,
     level: levelOf(record.run.requestedLevel, state.narrowed),
     policy,
     tampered: verdict.ok ? [] : verdict.tampered.map((t) => ({ ...t })),
     grants: state.approvals,
     protectedPathsTouched: escalations.protectedPathsTouched,
     tamperFindings: escalations.tamperFindings,
     ...(floor === undefined ? {} : { draft: floor }),
   });
   if (!next.ok) {
     if (next.reason !== 'approval-required') return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: next }, source: 'workspace' };
     const draft = floor === undefined || !isDraftStation(step.from) ? null : await draftStanding(vault, state, step.from, floor);
     return { state, standing: { standing: 'awaiting-approval', key: next.key, escalations: next.escalations, draft }, source: 'workspace' };
   }
   if (M1_STATIONS.includes(next.next)) return { state, standing: { standing: 'open' }, source: 'workspace' };
   // Approved, and a pull request opened that is not yet merged: a drive merges it before the run is done (D-I1b-01).
   const integration = await readIntegration(vault, runId);
   if (integration.some((r) => r.kind === 'opened') && !integration.some((r) => r.kind === 'merged')) return { state, standing: { standing: 'open' }, source: 'workspace' };
   return { state, standing: { standing: 'passed' }, source: 'workspace' };
 }

@@ -340,47 +344,47 @@ function securityFieldProblems(c: Partial<Record<keyof SecurityCheckSpec, unknow
 /**
  * Every security check the manifest must pin and every image it may: a
  * `secret-scan` check of the secret scanner's image, and, where the stack
  * has a static-analysis scanner, a `static-analysis` check of its image,
  * each refused naming the kind (I5). A check pins no rules only where its
  * scanner has rules of its own.
  */
 export function securityProblems(
   checks: readonly CheckSpec[],
   scanners: { readonly secretScan: SecurityScanner; readonly staticAnalysis: SecurityScanner | null },
 ): RequestProblem[] {
   const { problems, report } = collector();
   const declared: Readonly<Record<SecurityCheckKind, SecurityScanner | null>> = {
     'secret-scan': scanners.secretScan,
     'static-analysis': scanners.staticAnalysis,
   };
   const pinned = new Set<SecurityCheckKind>();
   checks.forEach((check, i) => {
     if (check.kind !== 'secret-scan' && check.kind !== 'static-analysis') return;
     const at = `manifest.checks[${String(i)}]`;
     pinned.add(check.kind);
     const scanner = declared[check.kind];
     if (scanner === null) {
       report(`${at}.kind`, 'unbound', `${at} is a static-analysis check, and the base's stack has no static-analysis scanner to run it`);
       return;
     }
     if (check.image !== scanner.image) {
       report(`${at}.image`, 'not-pinned', `${at}.image is ${check.image}; a ${check.kind} check runs ${scanner.image}, the image declared for its kind`);
     }
     if (check.config === null && scanner.defaultConfig === null) {
       report(`${at}.config`, 'not-config', `${at}.config is null, and the ${check.kind} scanner has no rules of its own; the check must pin them`);
     }
   });
   for (const kind of ['secret-scan', 'static-analysis'] as const) {
     if (declared[kind] !== null && !pinned.has(kind)) {
-      report('manifest.checks', 'missing', `manifest.checks pins no ${kind} check; every run is scanned for it (R17)`);
+      report('manifest.checks', 'missing', `manifest.checks pins no ${kind} check; every run is scanned for it`);
     }
   }
   return problems;
 }

 /**
  * The trees a run is built over, owned by the runtime and by nothing else
  * (D-P6-02). A task never runs over `run.repo`, the author's working copy:
  *
- * - at admission the working tree is snapshotted as the run's **base**, and
- *   its digest is recorded in the admission record;
+ * - at admission the tree of the run's base commit is read from git's object
+ *   store as the run's **base**, and its digest is recorded in the admission
+ *   record. Never the working folder: what is untracked or ignored there
+ *   (`.env`, `node_modules/`) never reaches a model, and the bytes are the
+ *   commit's own, whatever line endings the checkout wrote (#148, #154);
  * - each build attempt gets a pristine **start** tree, base plus the diff
  *   accepted so far, and a **work** copy of it, which is what is mounted;
  * - when the task has ended its **own diff** is `work` against `start`,
  *   compared in process by the adapters' `diffTrees` — never by `git`, whose
  *   repository config an agent can write (D-P8-04);
  * - verification runs over a **tree** built from base and the cumulative
  *   diff, content-addressed by that diff's digest, and the next task starts
  *   from the latest one that passed.
  *

+ * writes under one outlives the task.

 /** The digest a bundle records for a diff: over its entries, sorted by path. */
 export function diffDigest(diff: readonly DiffEntry[]): string {
   const sorted = [...diff].sort((a, b) => (a.path < b.path ? -1 : a.path > b.path ? 1 : 0));
   return sha256(JSON.stringify(sorted.map(({ path, change, sha256: content }) => [path, change, content])));
 }

+/** Why the base commit could not become the run's base: the request's fault, so admission refuses it (I5). */
+export class BaseRefusal extends Error {
+  override readonly name = 'BaseRefusal';
+  constructor(reason: string) {
+    super(`workspace: ${reason}`);
+  }
+}
+
+function reasonOf(error: unknown): string {
+  return (error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)).replace(/^readiness: /u, '');
+}

+    reviewer: declaring(graph.reviewer),
+    ...(drafting === null ? {} : {
+      drafting: {
+        specDrafter: declaring(drafting.specDrafter), specCritic: declaring(drafting.specCritic),
+        testDrafter: declaring(drafting.testDrafter), testCritic: declaring(drafting.testCritic),
+      },
+    }),
+  };
+}
+
+const DECLARING = new WeakMap<Driver, Driver>();
+/** Each declaring wrapper, back to the driver it wraps, so wrapping again starts from the driver itself. */
+const UNDECLARED = new WeakMap<Driver, Driver>();
+
+/** `driver`, declaring `TEST_TOOLS` when it declares no tool itself; every other member is its own. */
+export function declaring<D extends Driver>(driver: D): D {
+  if (driver.declaredTools().length > 0) return driver;
+  const known = DECLARING.get(driver);
+  if (known !== undefined) return known as D;
+  const wrapped = new Proxy(driver, {
+    get(target, key, receiver) {
+      if (key === 'declaredTools') return () => TEST_TOOLS;
+      const value = Reflect.get(target, key, receiver) as unknown;
+      return typeof value === 'function' ? (value as (...args: unknown[]) => unknown).bind(target) : value;
+    },
+  });
+  DECLARING.set(driver, wrapped);
+  UNDECLARED.set(wrapped, driver);
+  return wrapped;
 }
 
 function approvedFigure(req: RunRequest): number | null {

===== packages/api/test/paid/examples.paid.test.ts =====
/**
 * The shipped example, run for real (R36a): `examples/` — the policy, the
 * roles, the rubrics, and the run files — exactly as shipped, on the composed
 * host with the Claude Code driver in every seat, drafting its spec from an
 * intent as the README's first run does, through every gate a person holds
 * at L1, to a pull request merged on the GitHub held in process that the
 * composed line's paid case uses (D-I1a-07). No file outside `examples/` is
 * written but the user repository's own `package.json` and lockfile.
 *
 * **These assertions call the model, so they cost money, and they need a
 * Docker daemon and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.** They fail without either and are
 * never skipped. CI runs them only under the `run-driver` label, once per
 * tree. The run's measured cost is printed, for the pull request.
 */
import { cp, mkdir, mkdtemp, readFile, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { dirname, join } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { hashedLockfile } from '@olympus-ai/adapters';
import { FakeGitHub } from '@olympus-ai/conformance/fake-github';
import { invariantTest } from '@olympus-ai/conformance/vitest';
import type { RunId } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { UsageRecord } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import { afterAll, beforeAll, expect } from 'vitest';
import { approveStation, composeHost, loadPolicyFile, parseGraph, readUsage, resumeRun, runStanding, startRun, worstCaseCost, type BuiltGraph, type RunOutcome, type RunRequest } from '../../src/index.js';

const EXAMPLES = join(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..', '..', '..', '..', 'examples');
const runId = 'paid-example' as RunId;
/** The README's smallest first run: the spec drafted from this intent. */
const INTENT = 'Add hello.txt at the repository root, saying hello.';

let base: string;
let github: FakeGitHub;
let components: BuiltGraph;
let outcome: RunOutcome;
let gates: string[];
let usage: UsageRecord[];

beforeAll(async () => {
  const key = process.env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY;
  if (key === undefined || key === '') throw new Error('the paid example suite needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY; it is never skipped');
  base = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'paid-example-'));
  const repository = join(base, 'repository');
  // A user's JavaScript repository, with the run files copied in as the README says; the commit holds only its own files.
  await mkdir(repository, { recursive: true });
  await writeFile(join(repository, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'user-repo', private: true }));
  await writeFile(join(repository, 'package-lock.json'), hashedLockfile('user-repo', []));
  await mkdir(join(base, 'store'), { recursive: true });
  github = new FakeGitHub({ token: 'paid-example-token' });
  await github.start();
  const baseCommit = await github.seed(repository);
  await cp(join(EXAMPLES, 'run'), join(repository, 'run'), { recursive: true });
  components = await composeHost({
    store: join(base, 'store'),
    repository,
    trees: join(base, 'trees'),
    modelKey: key,
    git: { repository: github.repository, baseBranch: github.baseBranch, token: 'paid-example-token', apiBase: github.apiBase },
  });
  const loaded = await loadPolicyFile(join(EXAMPLES, 'policy.yaml'));
  if (!loaded.ok) throw new Error(`the example policy does not load: ${JSON.stringify(loaded)}`);
  const request: RunRequest = {
    runId,
    baseCommit,
    requestedLevel: 1,
    workspace: repository,
    intent: { text: INTENT, from: 'principal' },
    artifacts: { spec: 'draft', acceptanceTests: ['run/acceptance.md'], verificationManifest: 'run/verify.json', taskGraph: 'run/graph.json' },
    policy: loaded.policy,
    roles: join(EXAMPLES, 'roles'),
    rubrics: join(EXAMPLES, 'rubrics'),
    components,
    approvedCostUsd: null,
  };
  // The figure `create` prints, approved exactly, as a person does.
  const graph = parseGraph(JSON.parse(await readFile(join(repository, 'run', 'graph.json'), 'utf8')), { runId, baseCommit, workspace: repository });
  if (!graph.ok) throw new Error(`the example graph does not parse: ${JSON.stringify(graph.problems)}`);
  outcome = await startRun({ ...request, approvedCostUsd: worstCaseCost(graph.graph, loaded.policy, ['spec']).usd });
  // Each gate the policy holds for a person, approved as `olympus-ai approve` does: a drafted exit by its draft's hash.
  gates = [];
  for (let gate = 0; gate < 6 && !outcome.ok && outcome.reason === 'refused' && outcome.transition.reason === 'approval-required'; gate++) {
    const { standing } = await runStanding(components.vault, runId);
    if (standing.standing !== 'awaiting-approval') break;
    gates.push(standing.key);
    const approved = await approveStation({ runId, key: standing.key, draft: standing.draft?.draft.sha256 ?? null, approvedBy: 'paid-example', vault: components.vault });
    if (!approved.ok) throw new Error(`approving ${standing.key} was refused: ${approved.message}`);
    outcome = await resumeRun({ runId, components });
  }
  usage = await readUsage(components.vault, await components.vault.readRunState(runId));
}, 2_400_000);

afterAll(async () => {
  await github.stop();
  await rm(base, { recursive: true, force: true });
});

invariantTest(
  'I5.shipped-example-merges-on-the-real-driver',
  "the shipped examples, unmodified, drafting the spec from the README's intent, run on the composed host with the Claude Code driver in every seat through each gate a person holds at L1 — the drafted spec, the tests, and the merge — to a pull request merged into the base branch; paid (#145, #148 to #152, #154)",
  async ({ annotate }) => {
    // The measured cost, for the pull request (R36a acceptance), as a test annotation the reporter prints.
    const metered = usage.filter((r) => r.reading.kind === 'metered');
    const cost = metered.reduce((sum, r) => sum + (r.reading.kind === 'metered' ? r.reading.costUsd : 0), 0);
    await annotate(`cost $${cost.toFixed(4)} over ${String(metered.length)} metered calls; gates ${gates.join(', ')}; ${metered.map((r) => `${r.taskId}@${r.station} ${JSON.stringify(r.reading)}`).join('; ')}`);
    expect(outcome, JSON.stringify(outcome).slice(0, 2000)).toMatchObject({ ok: true });
    expect(gates).toEqual(['spec:1', 'test-design:1', 'integrate:1']);
    const [pull] = github.pulls();
    expect(pull?.merged).toBe(true);
    if (pull?.mergeCommit == null) return;
    expect(github.head(github.baseBranch)).toBe(pull.mergeCommit);
    // What merged is the change the spec asked for, and not the run's own files.
    const files = github.files(pull.mergeCommit);
    expect(new TextDecoder().decode(files.get('hello.txt')?.bytes).trim()).toBe('hello');
    expect([...files.keys()].some((path) => path.startsWith('run/') || path.startsWith('.drafts/'))).toBe(false);
  },
);

 - `spec.md`: one shippable capability, in statements each of which a test can

 - `security.json`: the spec's security part. Either

   "publicInterface": "..." }`. `testDirectory` is where the framework finds
   acceptance tests, relative to the repository root. `publicInterface` says
   exactly what a test calls and how to reach it from a file in
   `testDirectory`: module paths, function or command names, inputs, outputs.
 - `questions.json`: `{ "assumptions": [{ "point": "...", "chose": "..." }],
   "openQuestions": ["..."] }`. List each point the intent left undecided and
   what you chose. List as an open question anything you could not decide
   without guessing what the person wants.
 
 ## What you must not do
 
 - Do not settle an open question yourself. A spec with an open question goes
   to a person; that is the right outcome, not a failure.
 - Do not write tests, change the code, or write anywhere else in the tree.
   Nothing else you write is read.
 - Do not reach for anything the session does not offer you.

   /** Moves the base branch to a new commit over the same tree, as a push by someone else would. */
   moveBase(): string {
     const head = this.head(this.baseBranch);
     const tree = (this.#object(head, 'commit')).tree;
     const moved = this.#commit(tree, [head], 'someone else pushed');
     this.#refs.set(this.baseBranch, moved);

   }
 
   /** Merges a pull request onto its base as it now stands, as a collaborator pressing the button would. */
   mergeAsSomeoneElse(number: number): string {
     const pull = this.#pulls[number - 1];
     if (pull === undefined) throw new Error(`fake GitHub: no pull request #${String(number)}`);
     return this.#merge(pull);
   }
 
   /** Points a pull request at another base branch, creating it at the current base head if absent. */
   retarget(number: number, base: string): void {
     const pull = this.#pulls[number - 1];
     if (pull === undefined) throw new Error(`fake GitHub: no pull request #${String(number)}`);
     if (!this.#refs.has(base)) this.#refs.set(base, this.head(this.baseBranch));
     pull.base = base;
   }
 
   head(branch: string): string {
     const sha = this.#refs.get(branch);
     if (sha === undefined) throw new Error(`fake GitHub: no branch ${branch}`);
     return sha;
   }
 
   hasBranch(branch: string): boolean {
     return this.#refs.has(branch);
   }
 
   /** Every file in a commit's tree, by path. */
   files(commit: string): Map<string, FileEntry> {
     const out = new Map<string, FileEntry>();
     const walk = (sha: string, prefix: string): void => {
       for (const e of this.#object(sha, 'tree').entries) {
         const path = prefix === '' ? e.name : `${prefix}/${e.name}`;
         if (e.mode === '40000') walk(e.sha, path);
         else out.set(path, { mode: e.mode, bytes: this.#object(e.sha, 'blob').bytes });
       }
     };
     walk(this.#object(commit, 'commit').tree, '');
     return out;
   }
 
   parents(commit: string): readonly string[] {
     return this.#object(commit, 'commit').parents;
   }
 
   pulls(): ReadonlyArray<{ number: number; headRef: string; merged: boolean; mergeCommit: string | null; mergedAt: string | null }> {
     return this.#pulls.map((p) => ({ number: p.number, headRef: p.headRef, merged: p.mergedAt !== null, mergeCommit: p.mergeCommit, mergedAt: p.mergedAt }));
   }
 
   #object<K extends GitObject['type']>(sha: string, type: K): Extract<GitObject, { type: K }> {
     const o = this.#objects.get(sha);
     if (o?.type !== type) throw new NotFound(`no ${type} ${sha}`);
     return o as Extract<GitObject, { type: K }>;
   }
 
   #blob(bytes: Uint8Array): string {
     const sha = sha1('blob', bytes);
     this.#objects.set(sha, { type: 'blob', bytes });
     return sha;
   }
 
   #tree(entries: ReadonlyArray<{ name: string; mode: string; sha: string }>): string {
     // Git orders a tree's entries by name, a subtree's name compared as if it ended in '/'.
     const key = (e: { name: string; mode: string }): string => (e.mode === '40000' ? `${e.name}/` : e.name);
     const sorted = [...entries].sort((a, b) => (key(a) < key(b) ? -1 : key(a) > key(b) ? 1 : 0));
     const body = concat(sorted.flatMap((e) => [encoder.encode(`${e.mode} ${e.name}\0`), Buffer.from(e.sha, 'hex')]));
     const sha = sha1('tree', body);
     this.#objects.set(sha, { type: 'tree', entries: sorted });
     return sha;
   }
 
   #treeFrom(files: ReadonlyMap<string, { mode: string; sha?: string; bytes?: Uint8Array }>): string {
     interface Dir { files: Map<string, { mode: string; sha: string }>; dirs: Map<string, Dir> }
     const root: Dir = { files: new Map(), dirs: new Map() };
     for (const [path, entry] of files) {
       const parts = path.split('/');
       const name = parts.pop() ?? '';
       let dir = root;
       for (const part of parts) {
         let next = dir.dirs.get(part);
         if (next === undefined) {
           next = { files: new Map(), dirs: new Map() };
           dir.dirs.set(part, next);
         }
         dir = next;
       }
       const sha = entry.sha ?? this.#blob(entry.bytes ?? new Uint8Array());
       dir.files.set(name, { mode: entry.mode, sha });
     }
     const write = (dir: Dir): string => this.#tree([
       ...[...dir.files].map(([name, e]) => ({ name, mode: e.mode, sha: e.sha })),
       ...[...dir.dirs].map(([name, d]) => ({ name, mode: '40000', sha: write(d) })),
     ]);
     return write(root);
   }
 
   #flat(tree: string): Map<string, { mode: string; sha: string; type: 'blob' | 'tree' }> {
     const out = new Map<string, { mode: string; sha: string; type: 'blob' | 'tree' }>();
     const walk = (sha: string, prefix: string): void => {
       for (const e of this.#object(sha, 'tree').entries) {
         const path = prefix === '' ? e.name : `${prefix}/${e.name}`;
         if (e.mode === '40000') {
           out.set(path, { mode: '040000', sha: e.sha, type: 'tree' });
           walk(e.sha, path);
         } else out.set(path, { mode: e.mode, sha: e.sha, type: 'blob' });
       }
     };
     walk(tree, '');
     return out;
   }
 
   #commit(tree: string, parents: readonly string[], message: string, who = 'Fake <fake@example.invalid> 0 +0000'): string {
     const text = [`tree ${tree}`, ...parents.map((p) => `parent ${p}`), `author ${who}`, `committer ${who}`, '', message].join('\n');
     const sha = sha1('commit', encoder.encode(text));
     this.#objects.set(sha, { type: 'commit', tree, parents: [...parents], message });
     return sha;
   }
 
   /** A merge commit of the pull request's head over its base's current head, as `merge_method: 'merge'` makes. */
   #merge(pull: Pull): string {
     const head = this.head(pull.headRef);
     const merged = this.#commit(this.#object(head, 'commit').tree, [this.head(pull.base), head], `Merge pull request #${String(pull.number)}`);
     this.#refs.set(pull.base, merged);
     pull.state = 'closed';
     // A time no runtime clock reads, so a record that carries it took it from GitHub (A-R2-01).
     pull.mergedAt = MERGED_AT;
     pull.mergeCommit = merged;
     return merged;
   }
 
   #pullView(p: Pull): unknown {
     const [owner] = this.repository.split('/');
     return {
       number: p.number,
       html_url: `https://github.invalid/${this.repository}/pull/${String(p.number)}`,
       state: p.state,
       merged_at: p.mergedAt,
       merge_commit_sha: p.mergeCommit,
       head: { ref: p.headRef, sha: this.#refs.get(p.headRef) ?? '', label: `${owner ?? ''}:${p.headRef}` },
       base: { ref: p.base },
     };
   }
 
   async #handle(req: IncomingMessage, res: ServerResponse): Promise<true> {
     const url = new URL(req.url ?? '/', 'http://fake.invalid');
     const method = req.method ?? 'GET';
     const authorization = req.headers.authorization ?? null;
     this.requests.push({ method, path: url.pathname, authorization });
     if (authorization !== `Bearer ${this.#token}`) return send(res, 401, { message: 'Bad credentials' });
     const prefix = `/repos/${this.repository}/`;
     if (!url.pathname.startsWith(prefix)) return send(res, 404, { message: 'Not Found' });
     const path = url.pathname.slice(prefix.length);
     const body = method === 'GET' ? null : (JSON.parse(await text(req)) as Record<string, unknown>);
     try {
       return this.#route(method, path, url.searchParams, body, res);
     } catch (error) {
       if (error instanceof NotFound) return send(res, 404, { message: error.message });
       throw error;
     }
   }
 
   #route(method: string, path: string, query: URLSearchParams, body: Record<string, unknown> | null, res: ServerResponse): true {
     let m: RegExpExecArray | null;
     if (method === 'GET' && (m = /^git\/ref\/heads\/(.+)$/.exec(path)) !== null) {
       const sha = this.#refs.get(m[1] ?? '');
       return sha === undefined ? send(res, 404, { message: 'Not Found' }) : send(res, 200, { ref: `refs/heads/${m[1] ?? ''}`, object: { sha, type: 'commit' } });
     }
     if (method === 'GET' && (m = /^git\/commits\/([0-9a-f]{40})$/.exec(path)) !== null) {
       const c = this.#object(m[1] ?? '', 'commit');
       return send(res, 200, { sha: m[1], tree: { sha: c.tree }, parents: c.parents.map((sha) => ({ sha })) });
     }
     if (method === 'GET' && (m = /^git\/trees\/([0-9a-f]{40})$/.exec(path)) !== null) {
       const sha = m[1] ?? '';
       const tree = query.get('recursive') === null
         ? this.#object(sha, 'tree').entries.map((e) => ({ path: e.name, mode: e.mode === '40000' ? '040000' : e.mode, type: e.mode === '40000' ? 'tree' : 'blob', sha: e.sha }))
         : [...this.#flat(sha)].map(([p, e]) => ({ path: p, ...e }));
       return send(res, 200, { sha, tree, truncated: false });
     }
     if (method === 'POST' && path === 'git/blobs' && body !== null) {
       if (body.encoding !== 'base64' || typeof body.content !== 'string') return send(res, 422, { message: 'bad blob' });
       return send(res, 201, { sha: this.#blob(Buffer.from(body.content, 'base64')) });
     }
     if (method === 'POST' && path === 'git/trees' && body !== null) {
       const files = new Map<string, { mode: string; sha: string }>();
       if (typeof body.base_tree === 'string') {
         for (const [p, e] of this.#flat(body.base_tree)) if (e.type === 'blob') files.set(p, { mode: e.mode, sha: e.sha });
       }
       for (const e of body.tree as Array<{ path: string; mode: string; sha: string | null }>) {
         if (e.sha === null) files.delete(e.path);
         else {
           this.#object(e.sha, 'blob');
           files.set(e.path, { mode: e.mode, sha: e.sha });
         }
       }
       return send(res, 201, { sha: this.#treeFrom(files) });
     }
     if (method === 'POST' && path === 'git/commits' && body !== null) {
       const author = body.author as { name: string; email: string; date: string };
       const who = `${author.name} <${author.email}> ${String(Math.floor(Date.parse(author.date) / 1000))} +0000`;
       this.#object(body.tree as string, 'tree');
       return send(res, 201, { sha: this.#commit(body.tree as string, body.parents as string[], body.message as string, who) });
     }
     if (method === 'POST' && path === 'git/refs' && body !== null) {
       const ref = String(body.ref).replace(/^refs\/heads\//u, '');
       if (this.#refs.has(ref)) return send(res, 422, { message: 'Reference already exists' });
       this.#object(String(body.sha), 'commit');
       this.#refs.set(ref, String(body.sha));
       return send(res, 201, { ref: body.ref, object: { sha: body.sha } });
     }
     if (method === 'GET' && path === 'pulls') {
       const head = query.get('head');
       const [owner] = this.repository.split('/');
       const found = this.#pulls.filter((p) => head === null || head === `${owner ?? ''}:${p.headRef}`);
       return send(res, 200, found.map((p) => this.#pullView(p)));
     }
     if (method === 'POST' && path === 'pulls' && body !== null) {
       const headRef = String(body.head);
       if (!this.#refs.has(headRef)) return send(res, 422, { message: 'head does not exist' });
       const pull: Pull = { number: this.#pulls.length + 1, headRef, base: String(body.base), state: 'open', mergedAt: null, mergeCommit: null };
       this.#pulls.push(pull);
       return send(res, 201, this.#pullView(pull));
     }
     if ((m = /^pulls\/(\d+)(\/merge)?$/.exec(path)) !== null) {
       const pull = this.#pulls[Number(m[1]) - 1];
       if (pull === undefined) return send(res, 404, { message: 'Not Found' });
       if (method === 'GET' && m[2] === undefined) return send(res, 200, this.#pullView(pull));
       if (method === 'PUT' && m[2] !== undefined && body !== null) {
         const head = this.head(pull.headRef);
         if (this.failMerge) return send(res, 405, { message: 'Pull Request is not mergeable' });
         if (pull.mergedAt !== null) return send(res, 405, { message: 'Pull Request is already merged' });
         if (body.sha !== head) return send(res, 409, { message: 'Head branch was modified' });
         const before = this.beforeNextMerge;
         this.beforeNextMerge = null;
         before?.();
         return send(res, 200, { merged: true, sha: this.#merge(pull), message: 'Pull Request successfully merged' });
       }
     }
     return send(res, 404, { message: `no route ${method} ${path}` });
   }
 }

===== packages/conformance/src/kit/git.ts =====
/**
 * A fixture as a commit. Admission takes a run's base from the tree of its
 * base commit, read from git's object store (D-R36a-04), so a fixture a test
 * admits a run over is committed first, and the run names that commit.
 *
 * The commit is a function of the tree alone: a fixed identity, fixed dates,
 * no parent, and `core.autocrlf=false`, so the blobs are the fixture's bytes
 * whatever the host's git config says, and committing the same tree twice
 * names the same commit. No ref is moved; the object is enough for
 * admission to read it. Test code only.
 */
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { existsSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join } from 'node:path';

const IDENTITY = {
  GIT_AUTHOR_NAME: 'fixture',
  GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL: 'fixture@example.invalid',
  GIT_AUTHOR_DATE: '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z',
  GIT_COMMITTER_NAME: 'fixture',
  GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL: 'fixture@example.invalid',
  GIT_COMMITTER_DATE: '2026-01-01T00:00:00Z',
};

export interface CommitOptions {
  /**
   * Whether files the fixture's own `.gitignore` ignores are committed too.
   * Defaults to true, so a fixture's base holds every file it lays out, as a
   * copy of it did; false commits what `git add -A` would, and no more.
   */
  readonly includeIgnored?: boolean;
  /** Commit text files with LF endings whatever the checkout holds, as `core.autocrlf=input` does. Defaults to false: bytes as they are. */
  readonly normalizeEndings?: boolean;
  /** Commit only these paths, leaving the rest of the directory untracked. Defaults to everything. */
  readonly paths?: readonly string[];
}

function git(dir: string, args: readonly string[], autocrlf = 'false'): string {
  return execFileSync('git', ['-C', dir, '-c', `core.autocrlf=${autocrlf}`, '-c', 'core.safecrlf=false', '-c', 'core.fsmonitor=false', '-c', 'core.longpaths=true', ...args], {
    encoding: 'utf8',
    env: { ...process.env, ...IDENTITY },
    stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
  }).trim();
}

/** Commits everything under `dir` and returns the commit's full object name. */
export function commitFixture(dir: string, options: CommitOptions = {}): string {
  if (!existsSync(join(dir, '.git'))) git(dir, ['init', '-q']);
  git(dir, ['add', '-A', ...(options.includeIgnored === false ? [] : ['-f']), '--', ...(options.paths ?? ['.'])], options.normalizeEndings === true ? 'input' : 'false');
  const tree = git(dir, ['write-tree']);
  return git(dir, ['commit-tree', tree, '-m', 'fixture']);
}

/**
 * Rewrites the working files of `dir` from `commit` as a checkout with
 * `core.autocrlf=true` writes them, on any OS: every text file with CRLF
 * endings, while the commit's blobs keep LF (#154).
 */
export function checkOutWithCrlf(dir: string, commit: string): void {
  git(dir, ['config', 'core.autocrlf', 'true']);
  git(dir, ['rm', '-r', '-q', '--cached', '.'], 'true');
  for (const file of committedFiles(dir, commit)) rmSync(join(dir, ...file.path.split('/')), { force: true });
  execFileSync('git', ['-C', dir, '-c', 'core.autocrlf=true', 'checkout', commit, '--', '.'], { stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'] });
}

/** One file a commit's tree holds, as git stores it: its mode, and its blob's bytes (a link's are its target). */
export interface CommittedFile {
  readonly path: string;
  readonly mode: string;
  readonly bytes: Uint8Array;
}

/** Every file in the tree of `commit` in the repository at `dir`, read raw from the object store. */
export function committedFiles(dir: string, commit: string): CommittedFile[] {
  const listing = execFileSync('git', ['-C', dir, '-c', 'core.fsmonitor=false', 'ls-tree', '-r', '-z', '--full-tree', commit], { encoding: 'utf8' });
  const files: CommittedFile[] = [];
  for (const record of listing.split('\0')) {
    if (record === '') continue;
    const tab = record.indexOf('\t');
    const [mode, type, oid] = record.slice(0, tab).split(' ');
    if (type !== 'blob' || mode === undefined || oid === undefined) throw new Error(`git kit: ${record} is not a blob entry`);
    const bytes = execFileSync('git', ['-C', dir, 'cat-file', 'blob', oid]);
    files.push({ path: record.slice(tab + 1), mode, bytes: new Uint8Array(bytes) });
  }
  return files;
}

         for (const d of gates) {

             throw new Error(`P14: a failed gate was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
           }
         }
         if (gates.length !== maxIterations) throw new Error(`P14: ${String(maxIterations)} failed iterations recorded ${String(gates.length)} gate-failed decisions`);
       });
     },
   },
