+  // R36a: every failed attempt is recorded by the line with its source, layer, and trimmed text, before the retry it spends
+  // or the park it leads to, and the park names the last one's layer (#149, D-R36a-05).
+  'attempt-failed': {
+    kind: 'scenario',
+    run: async () => {
+      const { STATION_CONTRACTS } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
+      const { runStanding, startRun } = await api();
+      await withLine('p14-attempt-', async (rig) => {
+        const refusal = Object.assign(new Error(`the CLI produced no session (exit code 1); stderr: ${'x'.repeat(600)}`), { name: 'DriverRefusal', layer: 'invocation' });
+        const components = await rig.components({ driver: await stubDriver({ failWith: refusal }) });
+        const parked = refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components)), 'R36a attempt');
+        const failed = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'attempt-failed');
+        const retries = STATION_CONTRACTS.build.retry.max;
+        if (failed.length !== retries + 1) throw new Error(`R36a: ${String(retries + 1)} failed attempts were recorded as ${String(failed.length)}`);
+        // Recorded in order; read back in any, so sorted by the count each carries.
+        failed.sort((a, b) => (a.decision.cause === 'attempt-failed' ? a.decision.failure.retries : 0) - (b.decision.cause === 'attempt-failed' ? b.decision.failure.retries : 0));
+        for (const [i, d] of failed.entries()) {
+          if (d.decision.cause !== 'attempt-failed' || d.taskId !== HELLO_TASK || d.station !== 'build') throw new Error(`R36a: recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d)}`);
+          const { failure } = d.decision;
+          if (failure.source !== 'driver' || failure.layer !== 'invocation' || failure.retries !== i + 1) throw new Error(`R36a: the failure was read as ${JSON.stringify(failure)}`);
+          if (!failure.untrustedDetail.startsWith('the CLI produced no session (exit code 1)') || failure.untrustedDetail.length > 401) {
+            throw new Error(`R36a: the failure's text was kept as ${String(failure.untrustedDetail.length)} characters starting ${JSON.stringify(failure.untrustedDetail.slice(0, 60))}`);
+          }
+        }
+        if (parked.transition.reason !== 'parked' || !parked.transition.message.includes("the last attempt failed at the driver's invocation layer")) {
+          throw new Error(`R36a: the park does not name the last attempt's cause: ${JSON.stringify(parked.transition)}`);
+        }
+        // What `status` and `events` show: the stopped standing carries the last failure, read from the Vault.
+        const { standing } = await runStanding(components.vault, rig.runId);
+        if (standing.standing !== 'stopped' || standing.lastFailure?.task !== HELLO_TASK || standing.lastFailure.failure.retries !== retries + 1
+          || standing.lastFailure.failure.layer !== 'invocation') {
+          throw new Error(`R36a: the stopped standing does not show the last failure: ${JSON.stringify(standing)}`);
+        }
+      });
+    },
+  },
   'tier-escalated': {
     kind: 'scenario',

 /** A stub driver for one seat, whose model the assertion sets, recording every request it is given. */
 async function seat(options: SeatOptions): Promise<ObservedSeat> {
   const { StubDriver } = await core();
   const inner = new StubDriver();
   const model: ModelIdentity = { provider: 'stub', family: options.family as ModelFamily, model: options.model ?? `${options.family}-model`, version: '0' };
   const reported: ModelIdentity = options.reportedFamily === undefined ? model : { ...model, family: options.reportedFamily as ModelFamily, model: `${options.reportedFamily}-model` };
   const requests: TaskRequest[] = [];
   const workspaces: Array<Record<string, string>> = [];
   return {
     id: inner.id,
     contractVersion: inner.contractVersion,
     requests,
     workspaces,
     provenanceId: () => inner.provenanceId(),
     capabilities: () => inner.capabilities(),
-    declaredTools: () => inner.declaredTools(),
+    declaredTools: () => RIG_TOOLS,
     resolveModel: () => model,
     relayRequest: () => null,
     runTask: async (req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> => {
       requests.push(req);
       if (options.sandbox !== undefined) workspaces.push(await workspaceOf(options.sandbox, req));
       const narrative = (await options.act?.(req)) ?? 'drafted as asked';
       const result = await inner.runTask(req);
       return { ...result, claim: { narrative, filesChanged: [] }, model: reported };
     },
     cancel: (taskId: TaskId) => inner.cancel(taskId),
     emitArtifacts: (roles: CompiledRole[], targetDir: string) => inner.emitArtifacts(roles, targetDir),
     on: (kind: DriverEvent['kind'], handler: (e: DriverEvent) => void) => {
       inner.on(kind, handler);
     },
   };
 }

===== packages/conformance/src/registry/first-run.ts =====
/**
 * R36a: the shipped example runs on the real driver. Each assertion is the
 * free half of one fix the first real use needed (#145, #148 to #152, #154),
 * and fails when that fix is reverted (I8). The paid half, the shipped
 * `examples/` through the real driver to a merged pull request, is in
 * `packages/api/test/paid/`.
 */
import { mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { cp, mkdir, readdir, readFile, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { BuiltGraph, RunOutcome, RunRequest } from '@olympus-ai/api';
import { runtime } from '../kit/assert.js';
import { checkOutWithCrlf, commitFixture, committedFiles } from '../kit/git.js';
import type { LocalAssertion } from '../kit/types.js';
import { workspaceRoot } from '../kit/workspace.js';
import { approveAndResume, toApproval, withIntegration } from './integration.js';
import { refusalOf, withLine } from './line.js';
import { withVaultDirs } from './local-vault.js';
import { drafting } from './drafting.js';

const api = async () => import('@olympus-ai/api');

function examples(): string {
  return join(workspaceRoot(), 'examples');
}

/**
 * A user's JavaScript repository with the example's run files copied in, as
 * the README says, committed: a `package.json` and a lockfile pinning
 * nothing, so its stack is scanned for static analysis (#145).
 */
async function userRepository(dir: string, edit?: (dir: string) => Promise<void>): Promise<string> {
  const { hashedLockfile } = await import('@olympus-ai/adapters');
  await mkdir(dir, { recursive: true });
  await cp(join(examples(), 'run'), dir, { recursive: true });
  await edit?.(dir);
  await writeFile(join(dir, 'package.json'), JSON.stringify({ name: 'user-repo', private: true }));
  await writeFile(join(dir, 'package-lock.json'), hashedLockfile('user-repo', []));
  return commitFixture(dir);
}

/**
 * The example's graph: the Claude Code driver in every seat, over a stub
 * sandbox it is never driven on, with the host's own scanners, so the images
 * the example pins are the ones checked and the tools it grants are checked
 * against what that driver declares.
 */
async function exampleGraph(dirs: { store: string; artifacts: string }, base: string): Promise<BuiltGraph> {
  const [{ buildGraph, localWorkspaceStore, SCANNERS }, { StubSandboxProvider }, { LocalVault }, { ClaudeCodeDriver }] = await Promise.all([
    api(), import('@olympus-ai/sandbox'), import('@olympus-ai/vault'), import('@olympus-ai/driver-claude-code'),
  ]);
  const sandbox = new StubSandboxProvider();
  const driver = new ClaudeCodeDriver({ provider: sandbox });
  const user = process.getuid === undefined ? { uid: 1000, gid: 1000 } : undefined;
  const root = join(base, 'trees');
  return buildGraph({
    vault: new LocalVault(dirs), sandbox, driver, reviewer: driver,
    drafting: { specDrafter: driver, specCritic: driver, testDrafter: driver, testCritic: driver },
    workspaces: localWorkspaceStore(user === undefined ? { root } : { root, user }),
    scanners: SCANNERS,
  });
}

/** A create of the example's two runs, as the README's "First run" gives them, at L1 and with no figure approved yet. */
async function exampleCreate(policyFile: string, drafted: boolean, body: (outcome: RunOutcome, what: string) => void, edit?: (dir: string) => Promise<void>): Promise<void> {
  const { loadPolicyFile, startRun } = await api();
  const loaded = await loadPolicyFile(policyFile);
  if (!loaded.ok) throw new Error(`R36a: the policy does not load: ${JSON.stringify(loaded)}`);
  await withVaultDirs(`r36a-example-${drafted ? 'drafted' : 'supplied'}-`, async (dirs, base) => {
    const baseCommit = await userRepository(dirs.artifacts, edit);
    const req: RunRequest = {
      runId: `r36a-example-${drafted ? 'drafted' : 'supplied'}` as RunRequest['runId'],
      baseCommit, requestedLevel: 1, workspace: dirs.artifacts, policy: loaded.policy,
      roles: join(examples(), 'roles'), rubrics: join(examples(), 'rubrics'),
      components: await exampleGraph(dirs, base), approvedCostUsd: null,
      ...(drafted
        ? { intent: { text: 'Add hello.txt at the repository root, saying hello.', from: 'principal' }, artifacts: { spec: 'draft', acceptanceTests: ['acceptance.md'], verificationManifest: 'verify.json', taskGraph: 'graph.json' } }
        : { artifacts: { spec: ['spec.md'], acceptanceTests: ['acceptance.md'], verificationManifest: 'verify.json', taskGraph: 'graph.json', specParts: { security: 'security.json' } } }),
    };
    body(await startRun(req), drafted ? 'the drafted run' : 'the supplied run');
  });
}

export const EXAMPLE_ADMITTED_AS_SHIPPED: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.shipped-example-admitted-as-shipped',
  title:
    "the shipped examples — policy, roles, rubrics, and run files, unmodified — pass every refusal admission makes on a JavaScript repository, with the Claude Code driver's own tool declaration in every seat, and the only answer left is the worst-case cost to approve; both runs the README's first run gives (#145, #150)",
  run: async () => {
    for (const drafted of [false, true]) {
      await exampleCreate(join(examples(), 'policy.yaml'), drafted, (outcome, what) => {
        if (outcome.ok || outcome.reason !== 'cost-unapproved') throw new Error(`R36a: ${what} on the shipped examples was answered ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}, not with the cost to approve`);
      });
    }
  },
});

export const EXAMPLE_TOOLS_DECLARED: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I4.example-tools-declared-by-the-seat-driver',
  title:
    "every tool the shipped example policy grants any role is one the Claude Code driver declares, the driver every seat of the default host resolves to, so no seat is refused at drive time for a name it was granted (#150)",
  run: async () => {
    const [{ loadPolicyFile }, { DECLARED_TOOLS }] = await Promise.all([api(), import('@olympus-ai/driver-claude-code')]);
    const loaded = await loadPolicyFile(join(examples(), 'policy.yaml'));
    if (!loaded.ok) throw new Error('R36a: the example policy does not load');
    const roles = Object.entries(loaded.policy.roles);
    if (roles.length === 0) throw new Error('R36a control: the example policy declares no role');
    const undeclared = roles.flatMap(([role, scope]) => scope.tools.filter((tool) => !DECLARED_TOOLS.includes(tool)).map((tool) => `${role} -> ${tool}`));
    if (undeclared.length > 0) throw new Error(`R36a: the example grants tools the Claude Code driver does not declare: ${undeclared.join(', ')}`);
  },
});

export const UNDECLARED_TOOL_REFUSED_BEFORE_THE_ESTIMATE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I4.tool-grant-undeclared-by-the-seat-driver-refused',
  title:
    "a run whose policy grants a seat a tool its driver does not declare — the example's builder granted 'read' where the Claude Code driver declares 'Read' — is refused at admission, naming the role, the station, and the tool, before the worst-case cost is offered, so no spend is approved for a run whose every call would be refused (I4, I5, #150)",
  run: async () => {
    await withVaultDirs('r36a-undeclared-', async (_dirs, base) => {
      const policyFile = join(base, 'policy.yaml');
      const text = await readFile(join(examples(), 'policy.yaml'), 'utf8');
      const lowered = text.replace('tools: [Read, Write]', 'tools: [read, Write]');
      if (lowered === text) throw new Error('R36a control: the example policy grants no [Read, Write] to lower');
      await writeFile(policyFile, lowered);
      await exampleCreate(policyFile, false, (outcome) => {
        if (outcome.ok || outcome.reason !== 'policy-refused' || outcome.refusal.reason !== 'capability-missing' || outcome.role !== 'builder' || outcome.station !== 'build'
          || !outcome.refusal.detail.includes("'read'")) {
          throw new Error(`R36a: a grant of 'read' to the builder was answered ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}, not refused at admission before the estimate`);
        }
      });
    });
  },
});

/** Product code a checkout holds, with more than one line, so its line endings are what a checkout rewrites. */
const PRODUCT = { 'src/existing.ts': 'export const one = 1;\nexport const two = 2;\n' };

export const CRLF_CHECKOUT_MERGES: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.base-is-the-commit-under-autocrlf',
  title:
    "a working copy checked out with core.autocrlf=true, whose committed text files hold CRLF endings the commit's blobs do not, carries a run to a merged pull request, its run files uncommitted as the README has them: the base is the commit's tree read from git's object store, so integrate's comparison of the base with the commit holds; and an artifact committed with other bytes than the workspace holds is refused at admission, before the cost is offered, never at integrate (#154)",
  run: async () => {
    const checkout = (dir: string): string => {
      for (const [path, text] of Object.entries(PRODUCT)) {
        mkdirSync(join(dir, ...path.split('/').slice(0, -1)), { recursive: true });
        writeFileSync(join(dir, ...path.split('/')), text);
      }
      const commit = commitFixture(dir, { normalizeEndings: true, paths: Object.keys(PRODUCT) });
      checkOutWithCrlf(dir, commit);
      return commit;
    };
    await withIntegration('r36a-crlf-', async (it) => {
      // Control: the checkout really differs from the commit, as on a default Windows checkout.
      const onDisk = await readFile(join(it.rig.dirs.artifacts, 'src', 'existing.ts'), 'utf8');
      if (!onDisk.includes('\r\n')) throw new Error('R36a control: the autocrlf checkout wrote LF endings, so it proves nothing');
      await toApproval(it);
      const outcome = await approveAndResume(it);
      if (!outcome.ok) throw new Error(`R36a: a run over a CRLF checkout did not merge: ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}`);
    }, undefined, checkout);
    // The run's own files committed in that checkout: their bytes on disk are not the commit's, and the run is refused before the estimate.
    await withLine('r36a-crlf-artifacts-', async (rig) => {
      const { startRun } = await api();
      const components = await rig.components();
      const req = await rig.request(components, { approvedCostUsd: null });
      const commit = commitFixture(rig.dirs.artifacts, { normalizeEndings: true });
      checkOutWithCrlf(rig.dirs.artifacts, commit);
      const outcome = await startRun({ ...req, baseCommit: commit });
      if (outcome.ok || outcome.reason !== 'invalid-request' || !outcome.problems.some((p) => p.code === 'differs-from-commit')) {
        throw new Error(`R36a: an artifact committed with other line endings was answered ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}, not refused at admission`);
      }
    });
  },
});

/** Every path under `root`, files and directories alike, by its path relative to it. */
async function everyPath(root: string, at = ''): Promise<string[]> {
  const out: string[] = [];
  for (const entry of await readdir(join(root, ...at.split('/').filter(Boolean)), { withFileTypes: true }).catch(() => [])) {
    const path = at === '' ? entry.name : `${at}/${entry.name}`;
    out.push(path);
    if (entry.isDirectory()) out.push(...(await everyPath(root, path)));
  }
  return out;
}

export const IGNORED_FILE_REACHES_NO_TREE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I4.ignored-file-reaches-no-tree',
  title:
    "a file the working copy holds and its .gitignore ignores — a .env — is in no tree the runtime builds for a run, base, task, or check, and no sandbox is handed it, because the base is the commit and not the working folder (#148)",
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r36a-ignored-', async (rig) => {
      const { startRun } = await api();
      const components = await rig.components();
      const req = await rig.request(components);
      // Written after the rig's own commit, which adds ignored files too; a user's .env is never added.
      await writeFile(join(rig.dirs.artifacts, '.gitignore'), '.env\n');
      await writeFile(join(rig.dirs.artifacts, '.env'), 'API_KEY=never-in-a-tree\n');
      // The user's commit, made as git makes it: the ignored file is not in it.
      const baseCommit = commitFixture(rig.dirs.artifacts, { includeIgnored: false });
      if (committedFiles(rig.dirs.artifacts, baseCommit).some((f) => f.path === '.env')) throw new Error('R36a control: the commit holds the ignored .env');
      refusalOf(await startRun({ ...req, baseCommit }), 'R36a ignored');
      const trees = join(rig.workspaces.root, rig.runId);
      const paths = await everyPath(trees);
      if (!paths.some((p) => p.endsWith('/base') || p === 'base')) throw new Error('R36a control: the run built no base, so the search found nothing to find');
      const leaked = paths.filter((p) => p.split('/').includes('.env'));
      if (leaked.length > 0) throw new Error(`R36a: the ignored .env reached the run's trees at ${leaked.join(', ')}`);
    });
  },
});

export const DRAFTER_TOLD_WHERE_TO_WRITE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.drafter-request-names-its-drafts-directory',
  title:
    "the drafting task's request names the directory the runtime made for the run's drafts, .drafts/<run id>/, and the files the station reads from it, so a drafter granted no listing tool writes where the runtime collects (#151)",
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r36a-drafts-', async (rig) => {
      const r = await drafting(rig);
      const { startRun } = await api();
      await startRun(await r.request());
      const [first] = r.drivers.specDrafter.requests;
      if (first === undefined) throw new Error('R36a control: the spec drafter was never asked to draft');
      const dir = `.drafts/${rig.runId}/`;
      if (!first.variableSuffix.includes(dir) || !first.variableSuffix.includes(`${dir}spec.md`)) {
        throw new Error(`R36a: the drafter's request does not name ${dir}: ${JSON.stringify(first.variableSuffix)}`);
      }
      // Runtime text, in the suffix and never in the prefix the spec and rubric share.
      if (first.stablePrefix.includes(dir)) throw new Error('R36a: the drafts directory is in the stable prefix');
    });
  },
});

export const MANIFEST_REFUSED_BEFORE_THE_ESTIMATE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.manifest-refused-before-the-estimate',
  title:
    "a manifest admission refuses for what the base needs — the example's, with its static-analysis check removed, on a JavaScript repository — is refused at the first create, with no figure approved, rather than after the person approved the spend (#145)",
  run: async () => {
    const withoutStaticAnalysis = async (dir: string): Promise<void> => {
      const manifest = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(dir, 'verify.json'), 'utf8')) as { checks: Array<{ kind: string }> };
      const kept = manifest.checks.filter((c) => c.kind !== 'static-analysis');
      if (kept.length === manifest.checks.length) throw new Error('R36a control: the example manifest pins no static-analysis check to remove');
      await writeFile(join(dir, 'verify.json'), JSON.stringify({ checks: kept }));
    };
    await exampleCreate(join(examples(), 'policy.yaml'), false, (outcome) => {
      if (outcome.ok || outcome.reason !== 'invalid-request' || !outcome.problems.some((p) => p.code === 'missing' && p.message.includes('static-analysis'))) {
        throw new Error(`R36a: a manifest with no static-analysis check was answered ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}, not refused before the estimate`);
      }
      if (outcome.problems.some((p) => p.message.includes('(R17)'))) throw new Error('R36a: the refusal names an internal plan id');
    }, withoutStaticAnalysis);
  },
});

export const R36A_I4: readonly LocalAssertion[] = [EXAMPLE_TOOLS_DECLARED, UNDECLARED_TOOL_REFUSED_BEFORE_THE_ESTIMATE, IGNORED_FILE_REACHES_NO_TREE];
export const R36A_I5: readonly LocalAssertion[] = [EXAMPLE_ADMITTED_AS_SHIPPED, MANIFEST_REFUSED_BEFORE_THE_ESTIMATE, CRLF_CHECKOUT_MERGES, DRAFTER_TOLD_WHERE_TO_WRITE];

         if (!view.driving) return view;

   // Reached only if nothing above threw: a host that does not stop when its parent goes is a failure of its own.
   if (hung) throw new Error('I9: the headless host did not exit when its parent disconnected');

             throw new Error(`I4: the resolved scope at '${station}' carries tools [${granted.scope.tools.join(', ')}]; expected [${GRANTED_TOOLS.join(', ')}]`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.tools.includes(UNGRANTED_TOOL)) {
             throw new Error(`I4: the resolved scope carries '${UNGRANTED_TOOL}', which no role in the document grants`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.network.egress !== 'none') {
             throw new Error('I4: the resolved scope widened egress beyond what the document granted');
           }
         }
 
         // A grant is not a handle on the policy: widening what came back must
         // not widen what the next caller receives.
         const held = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (!held.ok) throw new Error('I4: the control resolution was refused');
         try {
           held.scope.tools.push(UNGRANTED_TOOL);
         } catch {
           // Frozen, which is the intended arrangement.
         }
         const next = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (!next.ok) throw new Error('I4: the second resolution was refused');
         if (next.scope.tools.includes(UNGRANTED_TOOL)) {
           throw new Error(`I4: mutating a resolved scope added '${UNGRANTED_TOOL}' to what the policy grants`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.omitted-approval-is-human-required',
       title:
         'resolvePolicy fills every approval the document omits with human-required and never auto, while carrying a stated approval through unchanged, so the filling is a default rather than a rewrite',
       run: async () => {
         const { APPROVAL_KEYS, AUTONOMY_LEVELS, STATION_IDS, StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const stated = APPROVAL_KEYS.filter((key) => document.approvals[key] !== undefined);
         if (stated.length !== 1) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the fixture document must state exactly one approval for this assertion to mean anything; it states ${String(stated.length)}`);
         }
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
 
         const expected = STATION_IDS.length * AUTONOMY_LEVELS.length;
         const keys = Object.keys(policy.approvals);
         if (keys.length !== expected) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the resolved policy carries ${String(keys.length)} approvals; the table must be total at ${String(expected)}`);
         }
         // Totality is proved by the count above; this loop is about what the
         // omitted keys were filled with.
         for (const key of APPROVAL_KEYS) {
           if (stated.includes(key)) continue;
           const outcome = policy.approvals[key];
           if (outcome !== 'human-required') {
             throw new Error(`I4: '${key}' was omitted by the document and resolved to '${outcome}'; an unconfigured approval must be human-required`);
           }
         }
         // The stated entry survives: a resolver that overwrote everything with
         // human-required would pass the loop above and be wrong.
         const [statedKey] = stated;
         if (statedKey === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the stated approval list has one entry and no entry in it');
         if (policy.approvals[statedKey] !== document.approvals[statedKey]) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the document stated '${statedKey}' and the resolved policy reports '${policy.approvals[statedKey]}'`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.empty-inventory-refuses-every-grant',
       title:
         'validateToolGrants against an empty inventory refuses every tool the policy grants, naming each with its role; an empty inventory is never read as allow-all',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine, validateToolGrants } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         const empty = validateToolGrants(policy, []);
         if (empty.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an empty inventory admitted ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length)} granted tool(s); an inventory offering nothing grants nothing`);
         }
         if (empty.ungranted.length !== GRANTED_TOOLS.length) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an empty inventory named ${String(empty.ungranted.length)} ungranted tool(s); expected all ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length)}`);
         }
         for (const tool of GRANTED_TOOLS) {
           if (!empty.ungranted.some((u) => u.tool === tool && u.role === GRANTED_ROLE)) {
             throw new Error(`I4: '${tool}' was granted to '${GRANTED_ROLE}' and the refusal does not name the pair`);
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a full inventory passes, so the refusal above is the
         // inventory doing work rather than the function refusing always.
         const full = validateToolGrants(policy, [...GRANTED_TOOLS]);
         if (!full.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an inventory covering every grant was still refused (${full.detail})`);
         }
         // And one short of the grants refuses exactly the missing one.
         const [first] = GRANTED_TOOLS;
         if (first === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the granted tool list is empty');
         const partial = validateToolGrants(policy, [first]);
         if (partial.ok) throw new Error('I4: an inventory missing a granted tool was accepted');
         if (partial.ungranted.length !== GRANTED_TOOLS.length - 1) {
           throw new Error(`I4: a partial inventory named ${String(partial.ungranted.length)} ungranted tool(s); expected ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length - 1)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.egress-entry-names-one-host',
       title:
         'an egress host list carrying a wildcard, a path, a prefix length, or whitespace is refused; a list of literal hosts is accepted, so the refusal is the entry being read and not the list being rejected',
       run: async () => {
         const { validatePolicyDocument } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Refusing the bare string 'all' while accepting ['*'] would leave the
         // authored form reading as an explicit allowlist while denoting every
         // host. Each of these denotes a set, or is not a host at all.
         for (const entry of ['*', '*.example.com', '0.0.0.0/0', '::/0', 'https://example.com', 'exa mple.com']) {
           const widened = { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, network: { egress: [entry] } } } };
           const outcome = validatePolicyDocument(widened);
           if (outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(
               `I4: a policy granting egress to ${JSON.stringify(entry)} was accepted. An egress list admits no ` +
                 'wildcard, so an entry that denotes a set makes a restrictive-looking policy unrestricted.',
             );
           }
         }
 
         // The control: literal hosts are accepted, so the check above is the
         // entry being read rather than every list being refused.
         const literal = { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, network: { egress: ['registry.npmjs.org', '192.0.2.10'] } } } };
         const accepted = validatePolicyDocument(literal);
         if (!accepted.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: a list of literal hosts was refused: ${accepted.defects.map((d) => d.problem).join('; ')}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.role-lookup-ignores-the-prototype-chain',
       title:
         'a role the policy does not define is refused with capability-missing even when Object.prototype carries a scope-shaped value under that name, and a role named for a prototype member refuses rather than throwing; the resolved role map has no prototype',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
         const prototype = Object.prototype as Record<string, unknown>;
 
         if (Object.getPrototypeOf(policy.roles) !== null) {
           throw new Error('I4: the resolved role map has a prototype, so an undefined role can be answered by it');
         }
 
         // Pollution somewhere else in the process must not become a grant here.
         prototype[UNDEFINED_ROLE] = { stations: ['build'], tools: ['read'] };
         try {
           const outcome = engine.resolveCapabilities(UNDEFINED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
           if (outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(
               `I4: role '${UNDEFINED_ROLE}' is defined nowhere in the policy and resolved to a grant because ` +
                 'Object.prototype carried that name. Default deny has to be total, and a lookup that reads the ' +
                 'prototype chain is not.',
             );
           }
           if (outcome.reason !== 'capability-missing') {
             throw new Error(`I4: the polluted role was refused as '${outcome.reason}'; expected 'capability-missing'`);
           }
           if (engine.resolveAutonomy(1, 'build', UNDEFINED_ROLE, policy).ok) {
             throw new Error('I4: resolveAutonomy granted a level to a role that exists only on Object.prototype');
           }
         } finally {
           Reflect.deleteProperty(prototype, UNDEFINED_ROLE);
         }
 
         // A role named for a prototype member must refuse, not throw: an
         // exception is not the refusal the contract promises, and a caller that
         // catches it has no reason recorded.
         for (const name of ['__proto__', 'constructor', 'toString', 'valueOf']) {
           const role = name as typeof UNDEFINED_ROLE;
           let refused: string;
           try {
             const outcome = engine.resolveCapabilities(role, 'build', policy);
             refused = outcome.ok ? 'granted' : outcome.reason;
           } catch {
             throw new Error(`I4: an ungranted role named '${name}' threw instead of refusing with capability-missing`);
           }
           if (refused !== 'capability-missing') {
             throw new Error(`I4: an ungranted role named '${name}' resolved as '${refused}'`);
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a real grant still resolves.
         if (!engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy).ok) {
           throw new Error('I4: the granted role was refused, so the guard is refusing everything');
         }
       },
     }),
     APPROVAL_OUTCOME_GATES_THE_STATION,
     external({

           }
           if (Object.hasOwn(outcome, 'level')) {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries a level; there must be nothing on it to mistake for a grant`);
           }
           if (outcome.detail === '') {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries no detail`);
           }
         }
 
         // The global cap, which the fixture document sets to the maximum and
         // so can never bite on its own. Lowered here, it must refuse a request
         // the station cap and role ceiling would both have allowed.
         const grounded = engine.resolvePolicy({ ...grantingDocument(), globalCap: 0, stationCaps: {} });
         const global = engine.resolveAutonomy(1, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded);
         if (global.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I5: L1 at 'verify' was granted at L${String(global.level)} under a global cap of L0`);
         }
         if (global.reason !== 'exceeds-cap') {
           throw new Error(`I5: a request above the global cap was refused as '${global.reason}'; expected 'exceeds-cap'`);
         }
         if (!global.detail.includes('global L0')) {
           throw new Error(`I5: the refusal does not name the bound that bit: ${global.detail}`);
         }
         if (!engine.resolveAutonomy(0, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded).ok) {
           throw new Error('I5: L0 under a global cap of L0 was refused; the cap is a ceiling, not an exclusion');
         }
 
         // The control: a request within every bound is granted, and granted at
         // the level requested rather than raised or lowered to a cap. An engine
         // that refused everything would satisfy the loop above.
         for (const level of [0, 1] as const) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, 'build', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           if (!outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' is within every bound and was refused (${outcome.reason}: ${outcome.detail})`);
           }
           if (outcome.level !== level) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' resolved to L${String(outcome.level)}; a request inside the caps is granted as asked, never adjusted`);
           }
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-document-refused-at-resolve',
       title:
         'resolvePolicy refuses a value that does not validate as a PolicyDocument, even though the parameter type says it is one, so a caller that asserts past the compiler cannot resolve a grant out of a malformed document; a genuine document still resolves',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I5: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Each of these is what a caller produces by writing `value as
         // PolicyDocument`. Without the guard, the first resolves a global cap
         // of 99 and grants every level; the rest widen a scope the document
         // never authorised.
         const rogues: Array<{ readonly what: string; readonly value: unknown }> = [
           { what: 'a global cap of 99', value: { ...document, globalCap: 99 } },
           { what: 'a role ceiling of 99', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, autonomyCeiling: 99 } } } },
           { what: 'a station id that is not one of the ten', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, stations: ['deploy'] } } } },
           { what: 'an unknown top-level key', value: { ...document, backdoor: true } },
         ];
         for (const { what, value } of rogues) {
           let refused = false;
           try {
             engine.resolvePolicy(value as never);
           } catch {
             refused = true;
           }
           if (!refused) {
             throw new Error(
               `I5: resolvePolicy accepted ${what}. The parameter type is a compiler claim, and a control that holds ` +
                 'only while every caller remembers to validate first is not closed.',
             );
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a genuine document resolves. A guard that threw on
         // everything would satisfy the loop above and break the engine.
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
         if (policy.globalCap !== document.globalCap) {
           throw new Error(`I5: a valid document resolved to globalCap ${String(policy.globalCap)}, expected ${String(document.globalCap)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-autonomy-level-refused',

           }

     runtime({
       id: 'I5.sandbox-egress-allowlist-enforced',
       title:
         'a sandbox provisioned with egress mode allowlist reaches the one host it names and no other, by name and by address; ' +

         'than read as deny-all or allow-all; and deny-all is unchanged, with --network none, no proxy, and no network created',
       run: async () => {
         await withProvider('i5-egress-allowlist-', async (provider, dirs) => {
           // An allowlist that names nothing is refused. It is not deny-all wearing the wrong
           // name, and it is not permission to reach everything; either reading would be the
           // silent degrade this invariant exists to refuse (D-P2-07, reversed by P10).
           const empty = await refusalFrom(() => provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [] } })));
           if (empty.layer !== 'egress') {
             throw new Error(`I5: an empty allowlist was refused at the ${empty.layer} layer, not the egress layer`);
           }
 
           // deny-all is untouched by the allowlist existing: no proxy is started beside a
           // sandbox entitled to no egress, and no network is created for it.
           await withSandbox(provider, specFor(dirs), async (handle) => {
             const controls = provider.appliedControls(handle);
             if (controls.egress.mode !== 'deny-all') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all spec was applied as ${controls.egress.mode}`);

               }

               // that only ever fires on a name is a DNS failure wearing an allowlist's clothes.

                 }
               }
 
               // The strongest form: the origin's own address, which the proxy demonstrably
               // reaches, refused for the one reason under test — the address is not on the list.
               const sameHost = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ HTTP/1.0`);
               if (!sameHost.includes('403') || sameHost.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: a reachable host was allowed by address although only its name was granted: ${sameHost}`);
               }
 
               // There is nothing to bypass. The internal network carries no default route, so a
               // process that unsets every proxy variable is left with no second path to take.
               const routes = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'ip route']);
               if (routes.stdout.includes('default')) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the sandbox has a default route, so the proxy is a convention rather than the only way out: ${routes.stdout}`);
               }

               }
             });
           } finally {

           }

           // host with no workload behind it and nobody watching it.

             ['network', egress.proxy.internalNetwork],

           ] as const) {

           }
         });
       },

 /** The CLI, by exact version. Never a range: a range makes the image a function of the day it was built. */

 /**
  * The tag the built image carries, derived from everything that decides what is
  * in it: the Dockerfile's bytes, the base digest, and the CLI version.
  *
  * A tag naming the CLI version alone does not identify the image. `ensureImage`
  * returns as soon as the daemon holds the tag, so changing the Dockerfile or
  * the base while leaving the CLI version alone left every machine that had
  * built the old one running it -- old runtime bytes under a report that hashes
  * the new source and blesses the result as current. Deriving the tag from the
  * inputs makes that impossible: different inputs are a different tag, and a
  * different tag is a build.
  */

 /**
  * The tool inventory: every tool this driver can offer a task
  * (`I4.driver-tool-inventory-validated`).
  *
  * A constant rather than a probe, because `validateToolGrants` runs before any
  * task starts and an inventory that needed a container to produce would make
  * the check depend on the thing it is supposed to gate. The constant is held
  * honest the other way round: an assertion starts the CLI with every tool
  * granted and requires the session to report exactly this set, so a CLI
  * upgrade that adds or removes a tool fails the suite instead of silently
  * widening or narrowing what policy can grant. That is why the version in
  * `image.ts` is exact and never a range.
  *

===== packages/vault/src/types.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/vault/src/types.ts b/packages/vault/src/types.ts

@@ -262,138 +262,160 @@ export type ResumeRefusal =
 /**
  * What a control decided, and which control it was. `decidedBy` is fixed by
  * the cause wherever one component alone can make it, so a decision cannot
  * be attributed to a component that does not make it. The runtime writes
  * every one of them on the decider's behalf: the station machine is pure and
  * the proxy is a container with no route to the Vault (D-P14-09).
  */
 export type DecisionCause =
   /**
    * The runtime closed a merged run's observation window: `windowMs` had
    * passed since `mergedAt`, by the runtime's clock (R8a, D-A-OB-03). The
    * run's `window-closed` observation is `observation`; no model and no
    * approval decided it.
    */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'observation-closed';
       readonly decidedBy: 'observe';
       readonly observation: string;
       readonly windowMs: number;
     }
   /**
    * The nightly re-hash found a record of this run whose bytes no longer
    * hash to its name or to the hash the last night saw, one a ref names that
    * is missing, or a lock that no longer verifies (A-R13a-04). `record` is
    * the store-relative path; nothing is repaired here, which is the
    * operator's.
    */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'record-drift';
       readonly decidedBy: 'nightly';
       readonly record: string;
       readonly problem: RecordDriftProblem;
       readonly expected: string;
       readonly observed: string;
     }
   | { readonly cause: 'admission-refused'; readonly decidedBy: 'admission'; readonly refusal: AdmissionRefusal }
   | { readonly cause: 'resume-refused'; readonly decidedBy: 'admission'; readonly refusal: ResumeRefusal }
   /** `station-machine` for a refusal `nextStep` or a capability check returned, `line` for one the line made itself. */
   | { readonly cause: 'station-refused'; readonly decidedBy: 'station-machine' | 'line'; readonly refusal: StationRefusal }
   /** `principal` is what the caller authenticated, as `ApprovalGrant.approvedBy` records it; `draft` the draft it approved, or null (R19). */
   | { readonly cause: 'approval-granted'; readonly decidedBy: 'approval'; readonly key: ApprovalKey; readonly principal: string; readonly draft: DraftRef | null }
   /** `key` is null when what the caller sent was not an approval key; it is not stored, since it is the caller's text. */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'approval-refused';
       readonly decidedBy: 'approval';
       readonly key: ApprovalKey | null;
       /**
        * `draft-mismatch`: the approval named another draft than the one
        * waiting. `draft-invalid`: the draft waiting carries problems the
        * runtime would refuse to lock, so it can be rejected and not
        * approved (R19).
        */
       readonly reason: 'invalid-request' | 'not-awaiting' | 'draft-mismatch' | 'draft-invalid';
     }
   /**
    * A person's rejection of a draft (R19). `notes` says whether the rejection
    * carried notes, which are the approver's text and are kept with the attempt
    * in run state, not here; `reason` is the fixed-set reason at `test-design`.
    */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'approval-rejected';
       readonly decidedBy: 'approval';
       readonly key: ApprovalKey;
       readonly draft: DraftRef;
       readonly principal: string;
       readonly reason: RejectionReason | null;
       readonly notes: boolean;
     }
   /**
    * A draft sent back to its drafter, decided by the line from what the
    * runtime recorded (R19): an independent critic's findings, the base run's
    * refusal of its checks, or the runtime's own problems with it.
    */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'draft-sent-back';
       readonly decidedBy: 'line';
       readonly draft: DraftRef;
       readonly sentBack:
         | { readonly by: 'critic'; readonly critic: VaultRef; readonly findings: number }
         | { readonly by: 'base-run'; readonly baseRun: VaultRef; readonly checks: readonly BaseRefusalPart[] }
         | { readonly by: 'runtime'; readonly problems: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly path: string; readonly code: string }> };
     }
   /** The drafted floor raised a drafted exit to a person, naming each condition that failed (R19, D-A-DS-04). */
   | { readonly cause: 'draft-floor-applied'; readonly decidedBy: 'line'; readonly draft: DraftRef; readonly level: AutonomyLevel; readonly failed: readonly DraftCondition[] }
   /** One connection the egress proxy decided, read from its log after it stopped. `host` is the agent's choice: data, never a prompt (I7). */
   | { readonly cause: 'egress-connection'; readonly decidedBy: 'egress-proxy'; readonly connection: EgressConnection }
   /** A violation already recorded; the decision points at it rather than copying it (D-P14-08). */
   | { readonly cause: 'violation-recorded'; readonly decidedBy: 'line'; readonly violation: VaultRef }
   /** A driver call whose relay refused anything, pointing at its usage record (D-P14-05). */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'relay-refused';
       readonly decidedBy: 'model-relay';
       readonly usage: VaultRef;
       readonly refused: number;
       readonly exhausted: Extract<MeterReading, { kind: 'metered' }>['exhausted'];
     }
   /**
    * A task's next iteration raised one tier under an escalation grant,
    * decided by the line from the failed gates run state counts, never from
    * anything a model returned (A-R14-02, I2).
    */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'tier-escalated';
       readonly decidedBy: 'line';
       readonly from: ModelTier;
       readonly to: ModelTier;
       readonly failedGates: number;
     }
   /**
    * `verify` found that a path `task` changed was also changed by the accepted
    * work of each task in `with` after `task` started. Decided by the line from
    * the trees the runtime built, before the conflict is committed, as a park
    * is; the task is rebuilt, or parks past the policy's bound (A-R4-03, I5).
    */
   /**
    * The run was narrowed (A-R10-03): at admission, by its author's trust or a
    * seated role's sensitive grant, or by the line, when a declared source's
    * input arrived. `scopes` is what each seated role narrowed to, and `level`
    * the ceiling every later exit reads its approval at. Recorded before the
    * narrowing is committed to run state (D-R10-03).
    */
   | {
       readonly cause: 'capability-narrowed';
       readonly decidedBy: 'admission' | 'line';
       readonly narrowing: NarrowingCause;
       readonly scopes: Readonly<Record<RoleId, NarrowedScope>>;
       readonly level: AutonomyLevel;
     }
   | {
       readonly cause: 'task-conflict';
       readonly decidedBy: 'line';
       readonly task: TaskId;
       readonly with: readonly [TaskId, ...TaskId[]];
       readonly paths: readonly [string, ...string[]];
-    };
+    }
+  /**
+   * A driver or sandbox attempt that failed, recorded by the line before it
+   * spends the retry or parks the task, so a park names what failed (#149,
+   * D-R36a-05). Nothing here is a status: the attempt produced no result, and
+   * the runtime's count of retries is what bounds it.
+   */
+  | { readonly cause: 'attempt-failed'; readonly decidedBy: 'line'; readonly failure: AttemptFailure };
+
+/**
+ * Why one attempt failed. `source` and `layer` are the runtime's reading of
+ * the error's type: a driver's refusal names its layer, a sandbox's its
+ * layer, and anything else is `error`. `retries` counts this attempt's
+ * failure. `untrustedDetail` is the error's own text, trimmed: it quotes the
+ * CLI's or the container's output, so it is stored and shown as data and
+ * never placed in a prompt (I7).
+ */
+export interface AttemptFailure {
+  readonly source: 'driver' | 'sandbox' | 'runtime';
+  readonly layer: string;
+  readonly retries: number;
+  readonly untrustedDetail: string;
+}
 
 export type DecidingComponent = DecisionCause['decidedBy'];
